> त्वं । एतानि । पप्रिषे । वि । नाम । ईशानः । इंद्र । दधिषे । गभस्तौ ।  
> अनु । त्वा । देवाः । शवसा । मदंति । उपरिबुध्नान् । वनिनः । चकर्थ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र त्वमेतानि नाम नामानि नामकान्युदकानि वि पप्रिषे । विपूरयसि
> हे इंद्र ईशानः सर्वस्येश्वरस्त्वं गभस्तौ हस्ते दधिषे धारयसि वज्रं धनं वा ।
> किंच त्वा त्वां शवसा बलेनोपेतं देवाः सर्वॆऽनु मदंति । अनुषुपुवंति । स त्वं
> वनिन उदकवतो मेघान्रश्मीन्वोपरिबुध्नानुपरिमूलानधोमुखांश्च कथ । कृतवानसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Indra, you fill up (वि पप्रिषे (*vi papriṣe*), glossed विपूरयसि (*vipūrayasi*)) these named waters (नाम (*nāma*), glossed नामानि नामकान्युदकानि (*nāmāni nāmakānyudakāni*)). O Indra, you, the lord (ईशानः (*īśānaḥ*)) of all, hold (दधिषे (*dadhiṣe*)) in your hand (गभस्तौ (*gabhastau*), glossed हस्ते (*haste*)) the thunderbolt or wealth. Moreover, all the gods rejoice in you, who are endowed with strength (शवसा (*śavasā*)). **NOTE** Such as you are, you have made (चकर्थ (*cakartha*), glossed कृतवानसि (*kṛtavānasi*)) the clouds (वनिनः (*vaninaḥ*)) that have water, or else the rays, to have their roots above (उपरिबुध्नान् (*uparibudhnān*)), that is, with their mouths turned downward."

### Printed p. 69

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- एतानि नाम (*etāni nāma*)—these waters
- वि पप्रिषे (*vi papriṣe*)—you have filled
- ईशानः (*īśānaḥ*)—you, the lord
- गभस्तौ (*gabhastau*)—in your hands the thunderbolt weapon
- दधिषे (*dadhiṣe*)—you possess
- शवसा (*śavasā*)—endowed with strength
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—all the gods
- अनु मदंति (*anu madaṃti*)—praise
- वनिनः (*vaninaḥ*)—the clouds that hold water
- उपरिबुधान् (*uparibudhān*)—as having their roots above
- चकर्थ (*cakartha*)—you have made

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you have filled all these waters. You, the lord, hold the thunderbolt weapon in your hands. All the gods praise you, who are endowed with strength. You have made the clouds that hold water have their roots above.

##### English translation
You have filled these waters, you, Indra, who are the lord (of all) bear (the thunderbolt) in your hands; the gods glorify you (who are endowed) with strength ; you have made the clouds with their root up- wards.
#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> च॒क्रं यद॑स्या॒प्सा निष॑त्तमु॒तो तद॑स्मै॒ मध्वच्च॑च्छद्यात् ।  
> पृ॒थि॒व्यामति॑षितं॒ यदूधः॒ पयो॒ गोष्पद॑धा॒ ओष॑धीषु ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> चक्रं । यत् । अस्य । अप्‌सु । आ । निसत्तं । उतो इति । तत् । अस्मै । मधु ।  
> इत् । चच्छद्यात् ।  
> प्रथिव्यां । अतिसितं । यत् । ऊधः । पयः । गोषु । आदधाः । ओषधीषु ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्येंद्रस्य चक्रमायुधं यदप्पु अंतरिक्षे निषण्णं उतो अपि च तदायुध
> मस्मा इंद्राय मधु चच्छद्यात् वशं नयति । यत्पृथिव्यामतिषितं निमित्तं. ऊधः
> ऊधसो मेघात् तदुदकं पयः गोष्टोषधीषु चादधा इति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The weapon (चक्रं (*cakraṃ*)) of this Indra that rests (निषण्णं (*niṣaṇṇaṃ*)) in the waters, that is, in the atmosphere (अप्सु (*apsu*)), that weapon brings the sweet (मधु (*madhu*)) into subjection (चच्छद्यात् (*cacchadyāt*), glossed वशं नयति (*vaśaṃ nayati*)) for this Indra. That which (यत् (*yat*)) is on the earth (पृथिव्यामतिषितं (*pṛthivyāmatiṣitaṃ*), glossed निमित्तं (*nimittaṃ*)), the udder (ऊधः (*ūdhaḥ*)), that is, from the cloud (मेघात् (*meghāt*)), that water, the milk (पयः (*payaḥ*)), you placed (आदधाः (*ādadhāḥ*)) in the cows and the plants. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 70

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- यत् (*yat*)—which
- चक्रं (*cakraṃ*)—weapon
- अप्पु (*appu*)—in the atmosphere
- आ निसत्तं (*ā nisattaṃ*)—abides, that is, is established or is discharged
- तत् (*tat*)—that weapon
- अस्मै इत् (*asmai it*)—for Indra alone
- मधु (*madhu*)—the sweet (and cloud-dwelling) water
- चच्छद्यात् (*cacchadyāt*)—will bring into subjection
- उतो (*uto*)—and
- पृथिव्यां (*pṛthivyāṃ*)—on the earth
- अतिसितं (*atisitaṃ*)—which is established
- ऊधः (*ūdhaḥ*)—obtained from the cloud (that is, of the rain)
- यत् पयः (*yat payaḥ*)—whatever water there is
- तत् (*tat*)—that water (O Indra, you)
- गोषु (*goṣu*)—in the cows (in the form of milk)
- ओषधीषु (*oṣadhīṣu*)—in the herbs and trees, that is, in the plant world
- अदधाः (*adadhāḥ*)—have established

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The thunderbolt of Indra, discharged by him in the atmosphere, brings the sweet water that is in the clouds into subjection for Indra. That is, with the help of his thunderbolt Indra will bring the water of the clouds under his control. O Indra, it is you who have established the water of the rivers, reservoirs and the like that are on the earth, the water that is in the cows in the form of milk, and the water that is in the herbs and trees in the form of sap.

##### English translation
When his thunderbolt abode everywhere in the firmament, it also subjugated the water to him ; the water which is let loose upon the earth puts milk into cows and herbs.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya for this Ṛk is not found in any book. Therefore the bhāṣya that is given in the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with Sāyaṇa's bhāṣya published by the Vaidika Saṃśodhana Maṇḍala of the city of Poona, taken from another book, is given here. Who its author is, is not clear. Although two or three other kinds of variant readings found in other books are also given there, since they do not appear to be very necessary, they are not written here. This Ṛk is recited in the Sāmaveda (Sā. 331) **NOTE**. The bhāṣya that Sāyaṇa has written on it is given here.
अस्येंद्रस्य चक्रमायुधमप्संतरिक्ष आ सर्वतो निषत्तं निषिण्णमासीन्मेघह ननार्थमुतो तदपि चास्मा इंद्राय मध्विदुदकमपि चच्छद्यात् वशं नयति । पृथिवामतिषितं विमुक्तं यदूधरुदकमस्ति तत्पयो गोष्टोष धीषु चादधाः आदधाति ॥ (*asyeṃdrasya cakramāyudhamapsaṃtarikṣa ā sarvato niṣattaṃ niṣiṇṇamāsīnmeghaha nanārthamuto tadapi cāsmā iṃdrāya madhvidudakamapi cacchadyāt vaśaṃ nayati | pṛthivāmatiṣitaṃ vimuktaṃ yadūdharudakamasti tatpayo goṣṭoṣa dhīṣu cādadhāḥ ādadhāti ‖*) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 71
#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अश्वा॑दिया॒येति॒ यद्वदं॒त्योज॑सो जा॒तमु॒त म॑न्य एनं ।  
> म॒न्योरि॑याय ह॒र्मेषु॑ तस्थौ॒ यतः॑ प्रज॒ज्ञ इंद्रो॑ अस्य  
> वेद ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्वात् । इयाय । इति । यत् । वदंति । ओजसः । जातं । उत । मन्ये । एनं ।  
> मन्योः । इयाय । हर्म्येषु । तस्थौ । यतः । प्रजज्ञे । इंद्रः । अस्य । वेद ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्रसामर्थ्यं दृष्ट्वा केचिदेनमश्वादादित्यादियायोदितवानिति वदंति
> यद्यद्यपि उत तथाप्यहमेनमोजसो बलाज्जातं मन्ये । जानामि । अस्य तेजस्वित्वं दृष्ट्वा सूर्यादुत्पन्न इति तेषां मतिः । अहं त्वोजःपदार्थाज्जात इति
> मन्ये यतोऽयं वृत्रादीन्हतवानिति । अथवायं मन्योः क्रोधादियाय ।
> उदितवान् । अतो हर्म्येषु शत्रुसंबंधिषु युद्धेषु हर्मैष्टेव वा तस्थौ ।
> तिष्ठति । यतोऽयं प्रजज्ञ उत्पन्न इतींद्र एवास्य । स्वस्येत्यर्थः । सामर्थ्यं वेद ।
> जानाति । न ह्यन्यो ज्ञातुमिष्टे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Seeing Indra's might, some say that he rose (इयाय (*iyāya*)) from the horse (अश्वात् (*aśvāt*)), that is, from the sun. Even so, I consider (मन्ये (*manye*)), I know, that he was born of strength (ओजसः (*ojasaḥ*)). Seeing his brilliance, their opinion is that he arose from the sun; but I think that he was born of the very substance called strength, because he slew Vṛtra and others. Or else he arose from anger (मन्योः (*manyoḥ*)). Therefore he stands (तस्थौ (*tasthau*)) in the houses (हर्म्येषु (*harmyeṣu*)) of the enemies, that is, in battles connected with enemies, or as it were in palaces. Because he was born (प्रजज्ञे (*prajajñe*)), Indra alone knows (वेद (*veda*)) his own might; for no other is able to know it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्वात् (*aśvāt*)—from the sun
- इयाय इति (*iyāya iti*)—that this Indra has become manifest
- वदंति यदुत (*vadaṃti yaduta*)—even if they say so
- एनं (*enaṃ*)—this Indra
- ओजसः (*ojasaḥ*)—from strength
- जातं (*jātaṃ*)—as one who has been produced
- मन्ये (*manye*)—I understand
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—this Indra
- मन्योः (*manyoḥ*)—from anger
- इयाय (*iyāya*)—has been produced
- हर्म्येषु (*harmyeṣu*)—in the houses and the like connected with enemies
- तस्थौ (*tasthau*)—stands
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra alone
- यतः प्रजज्ञॆ (*yataḥ prajajñè*)—from where he was produced
- अस्य (*asya*)—his own greatness
- वेद (*veda*)—knows

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Although people say that this Indra has been produced from the sun, I understand only that he is produced from strength. He is produced from anger, and he stands in the houses of the enemies. Whence he came is known to him alone. No one else can know his greatness.

### Printed p. 72

##### English translation
Although (some) say he came from the horse (the sun), I know that he is the offspring of strength ; he came from wrath, he stands in the homes (of his enemies) : Indra knows whence he was born.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Regarding the words ओजसो जातं (*ojaso jātaṃ*) in this Ṛk, Yāska has stated the Nirukta in this way.
यथो एतदोजसो जातमुतमन्य एनमिति चाहेत्ययमप्यग्निरोजसा बलेन मध्यमानो जायते तस्मादेनमाह सहसस्पुत्रं सहसः सूनुं सहसो यहुं ॥ (*yatho etadojaso jātamutamanya enamiti cāhetyayamapyagnirojasā balena madhyamāno jāyate tasmādenamāha sahasasputraṃ sahasaḥ sūnuṃ sahaso yahuṃ ‖*) (Ni. 4-7)
Although in the present Ṛk the epithets beginning with ओजसो जातं (*ojaso jātaṃ*) are applied to Indra, in general the epithet-words ओजसा जातं (*ojasā jātaṃ*), सहसस्पुत्रं (*sahasasputraṃ*), सहसः सूनुं (*sahasaḥ sūnuṃ*), सहसो यहुं (*sahaso yahuṃ*) are used in particular with reference to Agni. The word ओजः (*ojaḥ*) means strength. When Agni is said to be born of strength: when the priests produce Agni by churning in the fire-sticks, they have to use a great deal of their strength or force in churning. Because he is produced by churning with the exertion of force in this way, it has become the custom to call Agni ओजसो जातं (*ojaso jātaṃ*), born of strength; सहसः पुत्रं (*sahasaḥ putraṃ*), the son of strength; सहसः सूनुं (*sahasaḥ sūnuṃ*), सहसो यहुं (*sahaso yahuṃ*), the son of strength. To give an example, the present Ṛk itself may be called a mantra in which the words ओजसो जातं (*ojaso jātaṃ*) are used. Likewise, the Ṛk
द्र्वन्नः सर्पिरासुतिः प्रत्नो होता वरेण्यः । सहसस्पुत्रो अद्भुतः । (*drvannaḥ sarpirāsutiḥ pratno hotā vareṇyaḥ | sahasasputro adbhutaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-7-6)
for the use of the words सहसस्पुत्र (*sahasasputra*), and the Ṛk
त्वं ह यद्यविष्ठ्य सहसः सूननाहुतः । ऋतावा यज्ञियो भुवः ॥ (*tvaṃ ha yadyaviṣṭhya sahasaḥ sūnanāhutaḥ | ṛtāvā yajñiyo bhuvaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-75-3)

### Printed p. 73
for the use of the words सहसः सूनुः (*sahasaḥ sūnuḥ*), and the Ṛk
अग्ने वाजस्य गोमत ईशानः सहसो यहो । अस्मे धेहि जातवेदो महिश्रवः ॥ (*agne vājasya gomata īśānaḥ sahaso yaho | asme dhehi jātavedo mahiśravaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-79-4)
for the use of the words सहसो यहुः (*sahaso yahuḥ*), may be given as examples.
#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वयः॑ सुप॒र्णा उप॑ सेदु॒रिंद्रं॑ प्रि॒यमे॑धा॒ ऋष॑यो॒ नाथ॑मानाः ।  
> अप॑ ध्वां॒तमू॑र्णु॒हि पू॒र्धि चक्षु॑र्मुमु॒ग्ध्य१॒॑स्मान्नि॒धये॑व  ॒
> बद्धान् ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयः । सुपर्णाः । उप । सेदुः । इंद्रं । प्रियमेधाः । ऋषयः । नाधमानाः ।  
> अप । ध्वांतं । ऊर्णुहि । पूर्धि । चक्षुः । मुमुग्नि । अस्मान् । निधयाइव ।  
> बद्धान् ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयो गंतारः सुपर्णाः सुपतना आदित्यरश्मय इंद्रमुप सेदुः । उपसन्ना
> आभवन् । कीदृशाः । प्रियमेधाः प्रिययज्ञा ऋषयो द्रष्टारो नाधमानाः
> प्रज्ञां याचमानाः । याचनप्रकार उच्यते । हे इंद्र ध्वांतमंधकारमपोर्णुहि । परिहर । पूर्धि पूरय च चक्षुस्तेजः । मुमुग्नि मोचय चास्कान् निधयेव बद्धान् । निधा पाश्या भवति । पाश्या पाशसमूहः । पाशसमूहेन बद्धान्यथा
> मुंचंति तद्वत् । अत्र वयो वेर्बहुवचनमित्यादि निरुक्तं द्रष्टव्यं । नि. ४-३ ॥ ४ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The rays of the sun, which are goers (वयः (*vayaḥ*)) and good fliers (सुपर्णाः (*suparṇāḥ*)), that is, of good flight, approached Indra. What kind of rays? Those fond of the sacrifice (प्रियमेधाः (*priyamedhāḥ*)), seers (ऋषयः (*ṛṣayaḥ*)), who beg (नाधमानाः (*nādhamānāḥ*)) for wisdom. The manner of begging is now stated: "O Indra, remove the darkness, fill (पूर्धि (*pūrdhi*)) the eye with light, and release (मुमुग्नि (*mumugni*)) us, as if bound (निधयेव बद्धान् (*nidhayeva baddhān*))." A snare-mass (निधा (*nidhā*)) is पाश्या (*pāśyā*), a mass of snares; as people release those bound with a mass of snares, so here. Here the Nirukta beginning with the statement that वयः (*vayaḥ*) is the plural of वि (*vi*) is to be seen (Ni. 4-3).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रियमेधाः (*priyamedhāḥ*)—fond of the sacrifice
- ऋषयः (*ṛṣayaḥ*)—possessed of supersensory knowledge
- नाधमानाः (*nādhamānāḥ*)—able to obtain wisdom
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—moving
- सुपर्णा (*suparṇā*)—possessed of excellent extension: the rays of the sun
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- उपसेदुः (*upaseduḥ*)—have approached and become united (O Indra)
- ध्वांतं (*dhvāṃtaṃ*)—the darkness
- अप ऊर्णुहि (*apa ūrṇuhi*)—drive far away
- चक्षुः (*cakṣuḥ*)—the brilliance
- पूर्धि (*pūrdhi*)—fill
- निधयेव बद्धान् (*nidhayeva baddhān*)—us, who are as if bound with a snare
- मुमुग्गि (*mumuggi*)—release

### Printed p. 74

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The rays of the sun, fond of the sacrifice, possessed of supersensory knowledge, able to obtain wisdom, moving, and of excellent extension, have approached Indra and become united with him. O Indra, drive the darkness far away. Fill us with brilliance. Release us, who are as if bound with a snare.

##### English translation
The swift-moving well-flying (rays of the sun), loving sacrifices, discerning (the future), disserving knowledge, invested Indra ; dissipate the darkness, fill out the light; release us like men bound with a net.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Yāska has stated the Nirukta for this Ṛk thus:
वयो वेर्बहुवचनं । सुपर्णः सुपतना आदित्यरश्मिय उपसेदुरिंद्रं याचमानाः । अपोर्णुह्याध्वस्तं चक्षुः । चक्षुः ख्यातेर्वा चष्टेर्वा । पूर्धि पूरय देहीति वा । मुंचास्मान् पाशैरिव बद्धान् ॥ (*vayo verbahuvacanaṃ | suparṇaḥ supatanā ādityaraśmiya upaseduriṃdraṃ yācamānāḥ | aporṇuhyādhvastaṃ cakṣuḥ | cakṣuḥ khyātervā caṣṭervā | pūrdhi pūraya dehīti vā | muṃcāsmān pāśairiva baddhān ‖*)
The word वयः (*vayaḥ*) is the plural of the word वि (*vi*) (bird). सुपर्णः (*suparṇaḥ*), in the case of birds, means having beautiful wings. But since here this word is used with reference to the sun, the word means the beautiful or beneficial rays of the sun. Since the root वि (*vi*) has the sense of motion, the meaning of the word वयः (*vayaḥ*) is: having extension, that is, the rays of the sun that are coming towards us from the sun. Such beautiful rays of the sun approach Indra, praying to him. Drive far away the darkness that has spread all round. Fill the eyes with light, that is, grant to the eyes the power of seeing. As if bound with snares, that is, release us, who are without freedom, from our bonds: this is the main purport of the Nirukta.
The seventy-fourth sūkta.
## Sūkta 74 (Maṇḍala 10)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वसूनामिति षडृचं षष्ठं सूक्तं । ऋष्याद्याः पूर्ववत् । वसूनां षळित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*) has six Ṛks and is the sixth sūkta. The seer and the rest are as before. So it is recorded in the Anukramaṇī: वसूनां षळित्यनुक्रांतं (*vasūnāṃ ṣaḷityanukrāṃtaṃ*). The use in the ritual has been stated (गतो विनियोगः (*gato viniyogaḥ*)).

### Printed p. 75
Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*) is the sixth sūkta in the sixth Anuvāka. It has six Ṛks. The seer, deity and metre of this sūkta are the same as in the previous sūkta. In the Anukramaṇī it is said वसूनां षट् (*vasūnāṃ ṣaṭ*). The use in the ritual has been stated before.

Sūkta 74.
Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 74.
Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 3; Varga 5.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 6.
Seer: गौरिवीतिः (*gaurivītiḥ*).
Deity: इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*).
Metre: त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*).
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वसू॑नां वा चर्कृष॒ इय॑क्षन्थि॒या वा॑ य॒ज्ञैर्वा॒ रोद॑स्योः।  
> अर्व॑तो वा॒ ये र॑यि॒मंतः॑ सा॒त् व॒नुं वा॒ ये सु॒श्रुणं॑  
> सु॒श्रुतो॒ धुः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वसूनां । वा । चर्कृषे । इयक्षन् । धिया । वा । यज्ञैः । वा । रोदस्योः ।  
> अर्वंतः । वा । ये । रयिमंतः । सात् । वनुं । वा । ये । सुश्रुणं । सुश्रुतः ।  
> धुरिति धुः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इयक्षन् धनानि दातुमिच्छन्निंद्रो वसूनां धनानां लाभाय वा चर्कृषे ।
> अपकृष्यते । धिया वा । धीः कर्म धार्यमाणत्वात् । युद्धादिकर्मणा वा
> निमित्तेनाकृष्यते । यज्ञैर्वा निमित्तभूतैः कृष्यते । कैरिति उच्यते । रोदस्योर्द्यावापृथिव्योः संबंधिभिर्देवैर्मनुश्यैश्चत्यर्थः । सात् संग्रामे जेतव्ये सति
> येर्वंतो ना गच्छंतो रयिमंतो भवंति तैरप्याकृष्यते । ये वनुं वा
> हिंसां वा सुश्रुणं सुप्रसिद्धामत्यंतदुर्जयविषयां सुश्रुतः सुश्रवसः प्रसिद्धाः
> कुर्वंति तैरपींद्र आकृष्यत इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, wishing to give wealth (इयक्षन् (*iyakṣan*), glossed धनानि दातुमिच्छन् (*dhanāni dātumicchan*)), is drawn (चर्कृषे (*carkṛṣe*), glossed अपकृष्यते (*apakṛṣyate*)) for the gaining of riches (वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*)). Or he is drawn by धिया (*dhiyā*); धीः (*dhīḥ*) is action, because it is borne; he is drawn by a cause such as the action of battle. Or he is drawn by sacrifices as the cause. By whom? By the gods and men connected with the two worlds (रोदस्योः (*rodasyoḥ*), heaven and earth). When a battle (सात् (*sāt*)) is to be won, he is drawn also by those who, going swiftly (अर्वंतः (*arvaṃtaḥ*)), become possessed of wealth (रयिमंतः (*rayimaṃtaḥ*)). And Indra is drawn also by those who make violence (वनुं (*vanuṃ*)) well known (सुश्रुणं (*suśruṇaṃ*)), exceedingly hard to overcome, being themselves renowned (सुश्रुतः (*suśrutaḥ*)). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 76

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इयक्षन् (*iyakṣan*)—Indra, who wishes to give wealth
- धिया वा (*dhiyā vā*)—also on account of an action
- यज्ञैर्वा (*yajñairvā*)—or on account of a sacrifice
- रोदस्योः (*rodasyoḥ*)—by all the gods and men belonging to both heaven and earth
- वसूनां (*vasūnāṃ*)—for the gaining of wealth
- चर्कृषे (*carkṛṣe*)—is attracted
- सातौ (*sātau*)—in battle
- ये (*ye*)—who
- आर्वंतः (*ārvaṃtaḥ*)—moving about
- रयिमंतः (*rayimaṃtaḥ*)—are possessed of wealth, by them also
- ये (*ye*)—who
- सुश्रुतः (*suśrutaḥ*)—being renowned
- सुश्रुणं (*suśruṇaṃ*)—victorious
- वनुं वा (*vanuṃ vā*)—cause violence, by them also he is attracted

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who wishes to give wealth, is attracted, for the gaining of wealth, by all the gods and men belonging to both heaven and earth, on account of an action or on account of a sacrifice. He is attracted by all those who, moving about in battle, are possessed of wealth, and who, being victorious, cause violence to their enemies.

##### English translation
Indra desiring to bestow (wealth) is attracted by the inhabitants of heaven and earth (for the acquisitiou) of riches, either by pious acts or by sacrifices; (he is attracted) either by those swift-moving (persons) who acquire wealth in battle or by those who being successful (inflict) successful iujury.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> हव॑ एषा॒मसु॑रो नक्षत॒ द्यां श्र॑वस्य॒ता मन॑सा निंसत॒ क्षां ।  
> चक्षा॑णा॒ यत्र॑ सुवि॒ताय॑ दे॒वा द्यौर्न वारे॑भिः कृ॒णवं॑त॒ स्वैः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> हवः । एषां । असुरः । नक्षत । द्यां । श्रवस्यता । मनसा । निंसत । क्षां ।  
> चक्षाणाः । यत्र । सुविताय । देवाः । द्यौः । न । वारेभिः । कृणवंत । स्वैः ॥ २॥

### Printed p. 77

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एषामनुष्ठात्कृणामंगिरसां हव आह्वानशब्दोऽसुर इंद्रस्य प्रेरको द्यां
> दिवं नक्षत । व्याप्नोत् । तत्रत्या इंद्रसंबंधिनो देवाः श्रवस्यतान्नमिच्छता मनसा
> क्षां पृथिवीं निंसत । प्राप्तवंतः । यत्र यस्यां पृथिव्यां चक्षाणाः पणिभिरपहृता
> गाः पश्यंतो देवाः सुविताय सुष्टु हिताय स्मात्मनोऽभ्युदयाय दौर्नादित्य
> इव वारेभिर्वर्णनीयैः स्वैस्तेजोभिः कृणवंत प्रकाशमकुर्वन् । गवां प्रदानायांधकारमपनेतुमिति शेषः । अथवा । एषां यज्विनामपहृतानां गवां
> हवोऽसुर इंद्रस्य प्रेरयिता सन्नक्षत । इंद्रप्रेरिता अंगिरसः श्रवस्यतान्नं कीर्तिं
> वेच्छता मनसा क्षां भूमिं निंसत । अनुष्ठात्कृणां गाः प्रदर्शयितुमित्यर्थः ।
> इतरत्समानं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The call (हव (*hava*)) of these performers, the Aṅgirasas, which impels Indra, pervades (नक्षत (*nakṣata*)) heaven, that is, spreads through it. The gods there, who belong to Indra, with a mind desiring food (श्रवस्यता (*śravasyatā*)) have reached (निंसत (*niṃsata*)) the earth. On that earth the gods, seeing the cows carried off by the Paṇis, for their own good and for their advancement, like the sun, with their own splendours worthy of choice, made light (कृणवंत (*kṛṇavaṃta*)); what is to be supplied is: in order to give back the cows and to drive away the darkness. Or else: the call of these sacrificers, whose cows have been carried off, being the impeller of Indra, pervades; the Aṅgirasas, impelled by Indra, with a mind desiring food or fame, have reached the earth; the meaning is: in order to show the cows of the performers. The rest is the same.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—of these Aṅgirasas
- असुरः (*asuraḥ*)—impelling Indra
- हवः (*havaḥ*)—this invocation
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the world of the gods
- नक्षत (*nakṣata*)—pervades
- देवाः (*devāḥ*)—the gods
- श्रवस्यता (*śravasyatā*)—endowed with the wish for food
- मनसा (*manasā*)—with the mind
- क्षां (*kṣāṃ*)—the earth
- निंसत (*niṃsata*)—have approached
- यत्र (*yatra*)—on which earth
- चक्षाणाः (*cakṣāṇāḥ*)—those very gods who look upon the cows carried off by the Paṇis
- सुविताय (*suvitāya*)—for their own and the world's prosperity
- द्यौः न (*dyauḥ na*)—like the sun
- वारेभिः (*vārebhiḥ*)—attractive
- स्वैः (*svaiḥ*)—with their own splendours
- कृणवंत (*kṛṇavaṃta*)—have caused light

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The invocation of the Aṅgirasas, which impels Indra, pervades the world of the gods. The gods have approached the earth with a mind desirous of food. On the earth where they look upon the cows carried off by the Paṇis, those same gods, for their own and the world's prosperity, have caused light with their own attractive splendours, like the sun.

##### English translation
The animating invocation of these (Angirasas) pervaded heaven ; (the gods) with minds desirous of food kissed the earth : and there beholding (the lost cattle), the gods for their own benefit made (illumination) like the sun by their own glorious radiance.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒यमे॑षाम॒मृता॑नां॒ गीः स॒र्वता॑ता॒ ये कृ॒पणं॑त॒ रत्नं॑,

### Printed p. 78

> धियं च यज्ञं च साधंतस्ते नो धांतु वसव्यगमसामि ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इयं । एषां । अमृतानां । गीः । सर्वताता । ये । कृपणंत । रत्नं ।  
> धियं । च । यज्ञं । च साधंतः । ते । नः । धांतु । वसव्यं । असामि ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इयमेषाममृतानां यष्टव्यानां देवानां गीः स्तुतिः क्रियत इति शेषः ।
> ये देवाः सर्वताता सर्वतात् यज्ञे कृपणंत प्रयच्छंति याच्यंते वा रत्नं
> रमणीयं धनं । धियं चास्मदीयां स्तुतिं च यज्ञं साधंतः साधयंतस्ते
> नोऽस्मभ्यं वसव्यं वसुसमूहमसाम्यनल्पमसाधारणं वा धांतु । प्रयच्छंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This praise (गीः (*gīḥ*)) is made of these immortal gods, who are to be worshipped; "is made" is what is to be supplied. The gods who in the sacrifice (सर्वताता (*sarvatātā*)) give (कृपणंत (*kṛpaṇaṃta*)), or are asked for, delightful wealth (रत्नं (*ratnaṃ*)), while they accomplish our praise and our sacrifice, may those gods give (धांतु (*dhāṃtu*)) to us a mass of wealth (वसव्यं (*vasavyaṃ*)) that is abundant or incomparable (असामि (*asāmi*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- एषां अमृतानां (*eṣāṃ amṛtānāṃ*)—addressed to these gods
- इयं गीः (*iyaṃ gīḥ*)—this praise is offered
- ये (*ye*)—which gods
- सर्वताता (*sarvatātā*)—in the sacrifice
- धियं (*dhiyaṃ*)—our praise
- यज्ञं च (*yajñaṃ ca*)—and the rite
- साधंतः (*sādhaṃtaḥ*)—making fruitful
- रत्नं (*ratnaṃ*)—delightful wealth
- कृपणंत (*kṛpaṇaṃta*)—give
- ते (*te*)—they
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वसव्यं (*vasavyaṃ*)—a mass of wealth
- असामि (*asāmi*)—in great abundance
- धांतु (*dhāṃtu*)—may they provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

This praise is offered to the gods. May those gods who give delightful wealth in the sacrifice, making our praise and our rite fruitful, give us a mass of wealth in great abundance.

##### English translation
This (is) the praise of these immortal (gods), who bestow wealth at the sacrifice ; perfecting our praise and sacrifice, may they bestow upon us unequalled riches.
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ तत्त॑ इंद्रा॒यवः॑ पनंता॒भि य ऊ॒र्वं गोमं॑तं॒ तितृ॑त्सान् ।

### Printed p. 79

> सकृत्स्वं १ ये पुरुपुत्रां महीं सहस्रधारां बृहतीं  
> दुदुक्षन् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । तत् । ते । इंद्र । आयवः । पनंत । अभि । ये । ऊर्वं । गोमंतं । तित्य  
> त्सान् ।  
> सकृत्स्वं । ये । पुरुपुत्रां । महीं । सहस्रधारां । बृहतीं । दुधुक्सन् ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र ते तवायवो मनुष्या अंगिरसस्तत्तदा पनंत । आ सर्वतः पनंत ।
> सूयंते । येंगिरसो गोमंतमूर्वं संघं सणिभिरपहृतं प्राप्तं तितृत्सान्
> हिंसितुमैच्छन् ॥ तृदेर्हिंसार्थस्य सनंतस्य लेट्याडागमः । यद्योगादनिघातः ।
> अभ्यस्तस्वरः ॥ ये चांगिरसः सकृत्त्वं । या सकृत्सूते सा सकृत्वूः । तां
> सकृत्पजातां पुरुपुत्रां बहोषधिवनस्पतिरूपपुत्रां सहस्रधारां बहुलकामाना.
> मुत्पादयित्रिं बृहतीं विस्त्रतां महीं भूमिं । यद्वा । पुरुपुत्रां बहोषधिवनस्पतिरूपपुत्रां सहस्रधारां बहुकामवर्षित्रिं महीं महतीं पूज्यां वा
> बृहतीं परिमाणरहितां दिवं । दुधुक्षन् दुदुहुः । ते पनंतेति संबंधः सकृद्द
> द्यौरजायतेत्याद्युक्तं । ऋ. ६-४८-२२ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, your men, the Aṅgirasas, then praise (पनंत (*panaṃta*)) on every side; he glosses it as सूयंते (*sūyaṃte*). **NOTE** They are the Aṅgirasas who wished (तितृत्सान् (*titṛtsān*)) to strike (हिंसितुमैच्छन् (*hiṃsitumaicchan*)) the herd (संघं (*saṃghaṃ*)) full of cows (गोमंतमूर्वं (*gomaṃtamūrvaṃ*)) that had been carried off by the Paṇis and reached. And they are also the Aṅgirasas who milked (दुधुक्षन् (*dudhukṣan*), glossed दुदुहुः (*duduhuḥ*)) the earth: the one that bears only once is the once-bearing one (सकृत्वूः (*sakṛtvūḥ*)); her, who has many sons (पुरुपुत्रां (*puruputrāṃ*)) in the form of many herbs and trees, who is a producer of many desires (सहस्रधारां (*sahasradhārāṃ*)), vast (बृहतीं (*bṛhatīṃ*)), the great earth (महीं (*mahīṃ*)). **NOTE** Or else: she who has many sons in the form of many herbs and trees, who showers many desires, the great or worshipful earth, or the heaven, vast, without measure; they milked her. The connection is: those men praise. He adds that this has been stated in सकृद्द द्यौरजायतेत्याद्युक्तं (*sakṛdda dyaurajāyatetyādyuktaṃ*) (Ṛ. 6-48-22) **GLOSS**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- ये (*ye*)—which Aṅgirasas
- गोमंतं (*gomaṃtaṃ*)—full of cattle
- ऊर्वं (*ūrvaṃ*)—the herd (into the cow-pen)
- तितृत्सान् (*titṛtsān*)—those who wished to rush in and enter
- ये (*ye*)—which same Aṅgirasas
- सकृत्त्वं (*sakṛttvaṃ*)—that which came into being only once
- पुरुपुत्रां (*puruputrāṃ*)—that has sons of many forms
- सहस्रधारां (*sahasradhārāṃ*)—that furnishes desired things of many kinds
- बृहतीं (*bṛhatīṃ*)—that is very extensive
- महीं (*mahīṃ*)—the earth
- दुधुक्सन् (*dudhuksan*)—those who milked
- ते (*te*)—and who belong to you
- ते आयवः (*te āyavaḥ*)—those same human Aṅgirasas
- तत् (*tat*)—at that time
- आ (*ā*)—everywhere
- पनंत (*panaṃta*)—praise you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, the Aṅgirasas who wished to rush into the cow-pen full of cattle and enter it, and those same Aṅgirasas who milked the earth, which came into being only once, which has a progeny of herbs and the like of many forms, which furnishes desired things of many kinds and is very extensive, and who belong to you, those same Aṅgirasas
### Printed p. 80
praise you everywhere at the time of worship.

##### English translation
Your men praised (you) then, Indra, (those) who wished to break into the vast (stall) full of cattle; who milked the extensive once-generating earth, mother of many children, the showerer of thousands (of blessings).
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शची॑व॒ इंद्र॒मव॑से कृणुद्व॒मना॑नतं द॒मयं॑तं पृत॒न्यून् ।  
> ऋ॒भु॒क्षणं॑ म॒घवा॑नं सुवृ॒क्तिं भर्ता॒ यो वज्रं॒ नर्यं॑  ॒
> पुरुक्षुः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शचीऽवः । इंद्रं ! अवसे । कृणुध्वं । अनानतं । दमयंतं । पृतन्यून् ॥  
> ऋभुक्षणं । मघवानं । सुवृक्तिं । भर्ता । यः । वज्रं । नर्यं । पुरुक्षुः ।

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शचीवः कर्मवंतो यजमानाः ॥ व्यत्ययेनैकवचनं ॥ इंद्रमवसे
> रक्षणाय कृणुध्वं । कुरुध्वं। कीदृशमिंद्रं । अनानतं कदाचिदपि परेषामनवनतं
> पृतन्यून्पतना इच्छतः शत्रून्दमयंतं वशं प्रापयंतं ऋभुक्षणं महांतं
> मघवानं धनवंतं सुवृक्तिं सुष्टुतिं । यः पुरुक्षुर्बहुशब्द इंद्रो नर्यं
> नरेभ्यो हितं । असुरविघातित्वात् । तादृशं वज्रं भर्ता बिभर्ति तमिंद्रमवसे
> कृणुध्वमिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O men of ritual action (शचीवः (*śacīvaḥ*)), sacrificers, make Indra your protection (अवसे (*avase*)). What sort of Indra: one who has never bowed to others (अनानतं (*anānataṃ*)), who brings under his control (दमयंतं (*damayaṃtaṃ*)) enemies who desire battle (पृतन्यून् (*pṛtanyūn*)) **NOTE**, great (ऋभुक्षणं (*ṛbhukṣaṇaṃ*)), wealthy (मघवानं (*maghavānaṃ*)), well praised (सुवृक्तिं (*suvṛktiṃ*)); that Indra who is much sounding (पुरुक्षुः (*purukṣuḥ*), glossed as having many words) and bears the thunderbolt (वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)), which is beneficial to men (नर्यं (*naryaṃ*)) because it destroys the asuras; make that Indra your protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शचीवः (*śacīvaḥ*)—O sacrificers, men of ritual action
- अनानतं (*anānataṃ*)—who bows to no one
- पृतन्यून् (*pṛtanyūn*)—enemies
- दमयंतं (*damayaṃtaṃ*)—who subdues
- ऋभुक्षणं (*ṛbhukṣaṇaṃ*)—the great one
- मघवंतं (*maghavaṃtaṃ*)—the wealthy one
- सुवृक्तिं (*suvṛktiṃ*)—who has excellent praise (and)
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- पुरुक्षुः (*purukṣuḥ*)—who has much sound
- वज्रं (*vajraṃ*)—the thunderbolt
- भर्ता (*bhartā*)—who bears it
### Printed p. 81
- नर्यं (*naryaṃ*)—and who is beneficial to men
- इंद्रं (*iṃdraṃ*)—Indra
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protection
- कृणुद्वं (*kṛṇudvaṃ*)—make (him) yours

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O sacrificers, men of ritual action, make Indra your own for your protection: Indra, who bows to no one, who subdues enemies, the great one, the wealthy one, who has excellent praise, who has much sound, who bears the thunderbolt, and who is beneficial to men.

##### English translation
Celebrators of holy rites, select for your protection Indra the unsubdued, the tamer of hostile hosts; mighty, opulent, much glorified, who, loud-shouting, is the bearer of the thunderbolt, which is friendly to man.
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यद्वा॒वान॑ पुरु॒तमं॑ पुरा॒षाळा वृ॑त्र॒हेंद्रो॒ नामा॑न्यप्राः।  
> अचे॑ति प्रा॒सह॒स्पति॒स्तुवि॑ष्मा॒न्यदी॑मु॒श्मसि॒ कर्त॑वे॒ कर॒त्तत् ॥ ६ ।

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । ववान । पुरुतमं । पुराषाट् । आ । वृत्रहा । इंद्रः । नामानि । अप्राः ।  
> अचेति । प्रसहः । पतिः । तुविष्टान् । यत् । ईं । उश्मसि । कर्तवे । करत् । तत् ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> निष्केवल्ये यद्वावानेत्येषा धाय्या । सूत्रितं च । यद्वावानेति धाय्या
> पिबा सुतस्य रसिन इति सामप्रगाथः । आ. ५-१५ । इति ॥ महाव्रतेऽप्येषा । तथैव
> पंचमारण्यके सूत्रितं च । यद्वावानेति धाय्या सूददोहाः । ऐ.आ. ५-२-२ । इति॥
> यद्यदा पुरुतममत्यंतं प्रवृद्धतमं वृत्रं ववान हंति पुराषाट्
> शत्रुपुराणामभिभवितेंद्रः तदानीमानंतरमेव वृत्र हेंद्रो नामान्युदकान्यप्राः ।
> पूरयति ॥ व्यत्ययेन मध्यमः ॥ सोऽयमिंद्रः प्रासहः शत्रुवाणां प्रकर्षेणाभिभविता पतिः सर्वस्य स्वामी तुविष्टान् । तुवीति बहुनाम तत्सामर्थ्याद्धनं
> परिगृह्यते । बहुधन इत्यर्थः । ईदृशः सन्नचेति । सर्वैः प्रज्ञातः । स महानिंद्रो
> यदीं यदेतत्कर्मोश्मसि वयं कामयामहे तत्करत् । करोत्येव । इदं
> भवत्विदं भवत्विति यत्कामयामहे तत्तदानीमेव करोत्येव नोदास्त इत्यर्थः ।
> सैतदेव प्रत्यपद्यत यद्वावान पुरुतमं पुराषाळित्यादिकं ब्राह्मणमत्र द्रष्टव्यं ।
> ऐ. ब्रा- ३-२२ ॥
> xxix 6

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the Niṣkevalya this Ṛk is the dhāyyā. It is laid down in the sūtra: यद्वावानेति धाय्या (*yadvāvāneti dhāyyā*), पिबा सुतस्य रसिन इति सामप्रगाथः (*pibā sutasya rasina iti sāmapragāthaḥ*) (Ā. 5-15). This Ṛk is used also in the Mahāvrata; likewise it is laid down in the fifth Āraṇyaka: यद्वावानेति धाय्या सूददोहाः (*yadvāvāneti dhāyyā sūdadohāḥ*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-2). When (यत् (*yat*)) Indra, the overcomer of the cities of enemies (पुराषाट् (*purāṣāṭ*)), slays (ववान (*vavāna*)) Vṛtra, the greatest and most grown (पुरुतमं (*purutamaṃ*)), then straightway Indra the Vṛtra-slayer fills (अप्राः (*aprāḥ*)) the waters (नामानि (*nāmāni*), glossed as "waters"). This Indra, the overcomer of enemies in a high degree (प्रासहः (*prāsahaḥ*)), the lord of all, is possessed of much (तुविष्टान् (*tuviṣṭān*)); तुवि (*tuvi*) is a word for "much", and by its force wealth is understood, so the meaning is: possessed of much wealth. Being such, he is known (अचेति (*aceti*)) by all. That great Indra does (करत् (*karat*)) whatever act we desire (उश्मसि (*uśmasi*)): whatever we wish, saying "let this be, let this be", that he does at that very time; he does not neglect it. The Brāhmaṇa beginning सैतदेव प्रत्यपद्यत (*saitadeva pratyapadyata*) is to be seen here (Ai. Brā. 3-22). **GLOSS**
### Printed p. 82

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुराषाट् (*purāṣāṭ*)—Indra, who destroys the cities of enemies
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- पुरुतमं (*purutamaṃ*)—the exceedingly strong Vṛtra
- ववान (*vavāna*)—he destroyed
- आ (*ā*)—immediately after that
- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—the destroyer of Vṛtra
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- नामानि (*nāmāni*)—the waters
- अप्रा: (*aprā:*)—filled
- प्रासहः (*prāsahaḥ*)—who defeats enemies
- पतिः (*patiḥ*)—the lord of all
- तुविष्मान् (*tuviṣmān*)—Indra, who has abundant wealth
- अचेति (*aceti*)—is known by all
- यदीं (*yadīṃ*)—which fruit
- उश्मसि (*uśmasi*)—we desire
- तत् (*tat*)—that
- करत् (*karat*)—Indra accomplishes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who destroys the cities of enemies, when he destroyed the exceedingly strong Vṛtra, immediately after that, he, the destroyer of Vṛtra, filled the waters. Indra, who defeats enemies, the lord of all, who has abundant wealth, is known by all. May Indra accomplish whatever fruit we desire.

##### English translation
When Indra, the destroyer of cities, had slain the most mighty (Vritra), then he the Vritra-slayer filled the waters; he was manifest (to all), the subduer of enemies, the lord of all, the most opulent ; may he do that which we desire to have done.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

That this Ṛk beginning यद्वावान (*yadvāvāna*) is to be recited as the yājyā mantra among the mantras of the Niṣkevalya-śastra is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra यद्वावानेति धाय्या पिबा सुतस्य रसिन इति साम प्रगाथः (*yadvāvāneti dhāyyā pibā sutasya rasina iti sāma pragāthaḥ*) (Ā. 5-15). That this Ṛk has a use also in the Mahāvrata-iṣṭi is indicated in the fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence यद्वावानेति धाय्या सूददोहाः (*yadvāvāneti dhāyyā sūdadohāḥ*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-2).
## Sūkta 75 (Maṇḍala 10)
**Introduction**
Introduction to the seventy-fifth sūkta. In this sūkta some rivers, beginning with the river Sindhu, are praised. What rivers are is generally known to everyone. Yāska has explained the derivation of the word nadī in this way—
नद्यः कस्मान्नदना भवंति शब्दवत्यः (*nadyaḥ kasmānnadanā bhavaṃti śabdavatyaḥ*)
### Printed p. 83
नद्यः (*nadyaḥ*) means नदनाः (*nadanāḥ*): because they make a gurgling sound as they flow, they have received the name नद्यः (*nadyaḥ*); so he has said.
बहुलमासां नैघुंटुकं वृत्तमाश्चर्यमिव प्राधान्येन तत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । विश्वामित्र ऋषिः सुदासः पैजवनस्य पुरोहितो बभूव ! विश्वामित्रः । सर्वमित्रः । सर्व० संस्कृतं । सुदाः कल्याणदानः । पैजवनः पिजवनस्य पुत्रः । पिजवनः पुनः स्पर्धनीयजवो वामिश्रीभावगतिर्वा । स वित्तं गृहीत्वा विपाट्छुतुद्रोः संभेदमाययौ। अनुययुरितरे! स विश्वामित्रो नदीस्तुष्टाव । गाधा भवत इति । अपि द्विवदपि बहुवत् तद्यद्विपदुपरिष्कात्तद्व्याख्यास्यामः ॥ (*bahulamāsāṃ naighuṃṭukaṃ vṛttamāścaryamiva prādhānyena tatretihāsamācakṣate | viśvāmitra ṛṣiḥ sudāsaḥ paijavanasya purohito babhūva ! viśvāmitraḥ | sarvamitraḥ | sarva0 saṃskṛtaṃ | sudāḥ kalyāṇadānaḥ | paijavanaḥ pijavanasya putraḥ | pijavanaḥ punaḥ spardhanīyajavo vāmiśrībhāvagatirvā | sa vittaṃ gṛhītvā vipāṭchutudroḥ saṃbhedamāyayau| anuyayuritare! sa viśvāmitro nadīstuṣṭāva | gādhā bhavata iti | api dvivadapi bahuvat tadyadvipadupariṣkāttadvyākhyāsyāmaḥ ‖*) (Ni. 9-24) **NOTE**
Having said that there is a wonderful ancient history concerning the rivers, he describes how the seer Viśvāmitra praised the rivers Vipāś and Śutudrī and obtained their favour. This history, and the conversation that took place between the seer Viśvāmitra and these rivers, we have explained at length in the later part of this introduction.
Yāska has given the names of the thirty-seven words in the Ṛgveda that denote a river, thus—
अवनयः । यह्व्यः । खाः । सीराः । स्रोत्याः । एन्यः । धुनयः । रुजानाः । वक्षणाः । खादो अर्णाः । रोधचक्राः । हरितः । सरितः । अग्रुवः । नभन्वः । वध्वः । हिरण्यवर्णाः । रोहितः । सुसुरुतः । अर्णाः । सिंधवः । कुल्याः । वर्याः । उर्व्यः । इरावत्यः । पार्वत्यः । स्रवंत्यः । ऊर्जस्वत्यः । पयस्वत्यः । सरस्वत्यः । तरस्वत्यः । हरस्वत्यः । रोधस्वत्यः । भास्वत्यः । अजिराः । मातरः । नद्यः ॥ (*avanayaḥ | yahvyaḥ | khāḥ | sīrāḥ | srotyāḥ | enyaḥ | dhunayaḥ | rujānāḥ | vakṣaṇāḥ | khādo arṇāḥ | rodhacakrāḥ | haritaḥ | saritaḥ | agruvaḥ | nabhanvaḥ | vadhvaḥ | hiraṇyavarṇāḥ | rohitaḥ | susurutaḥ | arṇāḥ | siṃdhavaḥ | kulyāḥ | varyāḥ | urvyaḥ | irāvatyaḥ | pārvatyaḥ | sravaṃtyaḥ | ūrjasvatyaḥ | payasvatyaḥ | sarasvatyaḥ | tarasvatyaḥ | harasvatyaḥ | rodhasvatyaḥ | bhāsvatyaḥ | ajirāḥ | mātaraḥ | nadyaḥ ‖*) (Ni. 2-24)
All these are plural words denoting river-names. Their derivation and the meanings of their parts we shall explain when we write the work on the Nirukta.
Although it is said that all the rivers are praised in this sūkta, the praise of the river Sindhu is the chief part of it. Although the names of many rivers are indicated in the Ṛgveda, the names of the rivers Sindhu and Sarasvatī are recited most often. Sarasvatī has a twofold personality, as a river and as the goddess of speech belonging to the middle region (the atmosphere). In the present sūkta not all the names of the rivers indicated in the Ṛgveda are given. There are more than twenty-one names. Among them are Sindhu, Sarayu

### Printed p. 84
and Sarasvatī are the great rivers (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-64-9), that is, rivers with abundant water. Rasā is a very deep river (10-108-1, 2). In general it is the custom to call rivers by words of the feminine gender. But some rivers (Sarasvān and so on) are also called by masculine words, as nada. In the case of the river Sindhu there are only descriptions such as the names of some of its tributaries, its abundance of water, and its being a great river. But in the case of the river Sarasvatī there is a special account. Therefore we have explained it here at length.
Sarasvatī—Sāyaṇācārya has given a fivefold explanation of the meaning of the word Sarasvatī as used in the mantras of the Ṛgveda.
(1) River—Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-142-9; 1-164-52; 2-30-8 and so on.
(2) Name of a river—Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-3-12; 2-41-6; 3-23-4; in 6-41 the Ṛks 4, 7, 10, 11, 13; 6-52-6; 6-61-2; 7-36-6; 7-95-1 and 2; 7-96-1; 8-21-17 and 18; 10-64-9; 10-65-13; 10-75-5.
(3) Name of a goddess—Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-43-11; 5-46-2; 6-49. 7; 5-50-12; 10-17-7; 10-30-12 and so on. **NOTE**
(4) The presiding deity of the sky (one of the three goddesses Iḷā, Bhāratī and Sarasvatī)—Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-188-8 and so on.
(5) The goddess of speech—Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-1-11; 3-4-8; 3-54-13; 5-42-12; 7-39.5; 7-40-3; 9-97-32 and so on.
In the Ṛgveda there are many mantras that praise the goddess Sarasvatī. But the authors of the Nirukta have said nothing about who this goddess Sarasvatī is and what her nature is. They have said only नदीवद्देवतावच्च निगमा भवंति (*nadīvaddevatāvacca nigamā bhavaṃti*). That is, in some mantras that have Sarasvatī as their deity, Sarasvatī is described as a river, and in some mantras as a goddess. But it is not clear whether the river Sarasvatī itself is praised as being thought of as a goddess, or whether the goddess herself is praised as being thought of as a river. Therefore it has to be said that Sarasvatī denotes both a river and a goddess. On the view that Sarasvatī is a goddess, who is that goddess? What is her nature? What are the position and rank of that goddess? It is not easy to give a satisfactory answer to these and similar questions. On the view that Sarasvatī is a river, it can be said that a famous river of that name existed formerly (the present Sarasuti, the mordern Sursuti) and that it is that river which is praised in the Ṛgveda. In saying so, a doubt arises: in sacrifices and the like that are performed addressing certain definite deities
### Printed p. 85
is not the propounding of insentient things such as a river forbidden? In this matter Jaimini the great seer, in his Mīmāṃsā sūtra, has set out the prima facie view with अचेतनेऽर्थबंधनात् (*acetane 'rthabaṃdhanāt*) (Jai. Sū. 1-2-35); the commentators have given the answer by citing the Vedānta sūtra अभिमानिव्यपदेशः (*abhimānivyapadeśaḥ*) (Ve. Sū. 2 1-5). **NOTE** That is, in the many places in the Vedic mantras where insentient things are addressed, it is to be understood that the statement is made about the presiding deities of those insentient things (see Part 1, pages 380-381). Therefore, where a river is praised, it is to be understood that the presiding deity of that river is praised. In regard to this goddess Sarasvatī many hold many different opinions—
(1) In the Purāṇas and the like Sarasvatī is said to be the presiding deity of speech, Śāradā, of the nature of learning, the rider of the swan—that is, a special goddess who is the deity of speech, famous under the name Śāradā, of the nature of learning, and seated on a swan.
(2) Or they say that this goddess Sarasvatī is of the form of Āditya (the sun) as distinguished by a special limiting condition (upādhi). The limiting condition means the time of twilight. Therefore it is the opinion of some that Āditya himself, endowed with the light of the twilight, is here praised in the form of Sarasvatī.
(3) The word saraḥ is derived from the root sṛ, "to go, to move": सरः अस्मा अस्तीति अथवा सरः तेजो यस्या विद्यते सा सरस्वतीति भवति (*saraḥ asmā astīti athavā saraḥ tejo yasyā vidyate sā sarasvatīti bhavati*). That is, because she shines with a form of radiance, she is called Sarasvatī.
(4) Or: प्रातर्वाचां प्रवर्तिका, प्राणिनां स्वापपरित्यागेन स्वोचितव्यवहारेषु प्रवृत्तौ शब्दायमानतायाः प्रवृत्तेः, रात्रौ च वाचां निरोधका रात्रौ प्राणिनां निलायनिलीनत्वेन सुप्यमानतया शब्दविरामात् । एवं च शब्दस्य प्रवर्तिका निवर्तिका एषैव लोके सरस्वतीत्वेन व्यपदिश्यते नान्या ॥ (*prātarvācāṃ pravartikā, prāṇināṃ svāpaparityāgena svocitavyavahāreṣu pravṛttau śabdāyamānatāyāḥ pravṛtteḥ, rātrau ca vācāṃ nirodhakā rātrau prāṇināṃ nilāyanilīnatvena supyamānatayā śabdavirāmāt | evaṃ ca śabdasya pravartikā nivartikā eṣaiva loke sarasvatītvena vyapadiśyate nānyā ‖*). That is, in the morning, awakening from sleep, living beings are engaged in their several activities and dealings, such as speaking. At night, when living beings are absorbed in sleep, they have rest from dealings and the like, without actions such as speaking or making sound. Therefore, because she is the cause of the beginning and ceasing of speaking or making sound, this goddess of speech is named Sarasvatī.
### Printed p. 86
(5) As said before, since this goddess has the character of speech and the like, this goddess Sarasvatī is of the form of Uṣas. For that very reason this goddess Sarasvatī's name stands in the middle of the words that are names of the goddess Uṣas, namely सूनृता (*sūnṛtā*), सूनृतावती (*sūnṛtāvatī*), सूनृतावरी (*sūnṛtāvarī*) and the like. Here the word सूनृत (*sūnṛta*) means speech that is both pleasant and true. In the same way, Śaunaka, the author of the Bṛhaddevatā, and other authors of works have explained in various ways the matter of the goddess Sarasvatī.
Now let us consider to some extent in what meanings this word Sarasvatī is used in the Ṛgveda. In this matter three points chiefly have to be noted. (1) The present sūkta प्र सु व (*pra su va*) on which we are now commenting. (2) Other Ṛks that have Sarasvatī as their deity. (3) The seers who composed these Ṛks, and their descendants. We shall examine all three of these matters.—
In Ṛ. Saṃ. sūkta 1-3 the Ṛks 10, 11, 12 are mantras having Sarasvatī as deity. In the Ṛks 10 and 11 the river Sarasvatī itself is praised, thought of as a goddess. The commentator Sāyaṇācārya has explained Sarasvatī in the Ṛks 10-11 as the goddess of speech, and in the Ṛk 11 as the presiding deity of the river called Sarasvatī. The scholar Griffith has explained Sarasvatī as meaning the goddess of speech. But when the word पावका (*pāvakā*) is taken to mean "purifying with water", and when the word अर्ण (*arṇa*) is taken to mean a river, one has to say that Sarasvatī is a river or the presiding deity of a river. Some of the modern scholars hold the opinion that Sarasvatī is the river Sindhu. But since it is said that the hermitage of the seer Madhucchanda, son of Viśvāmitra, who composed this Ṛk, stood on the western bank of the river called the present Sarasuti in Kurukṣetra (Mahābhārata, Śalya Parva, chapter 43), there is room to suppose that the seer Madhucchanda praised this very river called Surasuti. For when praising a river, it does not seem right to say that he left the famous river near the place where he lived and praised another river of the same name somewhere far away. In the second Maṇḍala the Ṛks that have Sarasvatī as deity, Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-30-8; 2-32-8; 2-41-16 to 18, were all composed by the seer Gṛtsamada, son of Śunahotra. It is known that this seer Gṛtsamada was in the line of the seer Atri, the sixth generation from Atri (according to the work Pravara and Gotra, No. 25, printed in the Mysore Government Oriental Library). Viśvāmitra was the fifth generation from Atri. In eight Ṛks in all this seer Gṛtsamada has praised Sarasvatī.
### Printed p. 87
In Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-1-11 and 2.3-8 he has praised Sarasvatī together with Iḍā and Bhāratī. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 2 3-55 he has spoken of these three, that is, Iḍā, Sarasvatī and Bhāratī, as three goddesses. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-30-8 he has praised Sarasvatī together with Indra and the Maruts. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-32-8 he has spoken of Sarasvatī as a goddess. In the sixteenth Ṛk of Ṛ. Saṃ. sūkta 2-41 he has described Sarasvatī as a very great river and a very great goddess, and in the eighteenth Ṛk as ऋतावरी (*ṛtāvarī*), that is (ṛta, water), a river with abundant water. Seeing this, some of the Western scholars say that because she is called a very great river, it must be the river Sindhu. But that is not right. For when a poet praises a river or a goddess (even if it is small or ordinary) it is usual to describe it as very great. Some hold the opinion that in the Ṛk 2-30-8 this seer Gṛtsamada praises Sarasvatī, thinking of her as the presiding deity of thunder and lightning. But to say that one seer describes one and the same deity in different senses does not appear well founded. The seers of Ṛ. Saṃ. sūkta 3-23 are Devaśravā and Devavāta, sons of Bharata. According to the work on gotra and pravara mentioned before, it is known that these seers were sons of Viśvāmitra. It is known from the same work that Bharata was the sixth generation from Atri, that Viśvāmitra was the seventh, and that both were descendants of Purūrava, the great-grandson of Atri. In the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-23-4 they have said—
नि त्वा दधे वर आ पृथिव्या इळायास्सदे सुदिनत्वे अह्नां । दृषद्वत्यां मानुषा अपयायां सरस्वत्यां रेवदग्ने दिदीहि ॥ (*ni tvā dadhe vara ā pṛthivyā iḷāyāssade sudinatve ahnāṃ | dṛṣadvatyāṃ mānuṣā apayāyāṃ sarasvatyāṃ revadagne didīhi ‖*) (Pu.. Saṃ. 3-23-4) **NOTE**
Together with the river Sarasvatī they have named two other rivers, Dṛṣṭadvatī and Apayā. Dṛṣṭadvatī is the present river Citrāṅga (the modern Chitrang, Chautag or Chitang); Apayā is the present river Apagā (the modern Apaga). Since these two rivers are close to the river Sarasvatī, it can be said confidently that Sarasvatī is not the river Sindhu. Recent investigations have shown that the hermitage of the seer Viśvāmitra too (in Kurukṣetra) stood on the western bank of this river Sarasvatī. (See Dey's Geographical Dic.) Everything said so far concerns the descendants of the seers Atri and Viśvāmitra. Now let us examine the matter of Bharadvāja and his descendants. For the three Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-49-7; 6-50-12; 6-52 6, the seer is Ṛjiśvā, son of Bharadvāja.
### Printed p. 88
Of these three Ṛks, in the first and third Ṛks it is stated clearly that Sarasvatī is a river. In the second mantra, although the name of Sarasvatī is mentioned along with the deities Rudra, Viṣṇu, Vāyu and Parjanya, Sarasvatī can be taken to be the name of a river. For since that seer has said in two Ṛks that Sarasvatī is a river, it is natural that in another Ṛk the same seer has praised Sarasvatī as a river. The hermitage of Bharadvāja was at Prayāga (Allahabad). Therefore Ṛjiśvā, son of Bharadvāja, must have praised a river near his hermitage. The whole sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-61 of the seer Bharadvāja (14 Ṛks in all) is about Sarasvatī. Some Western scholars are of the opinion that the meaning of several words in this sūkta applies to the river Sindhu. But since those words apply to the river Sarasvatī as well, there is no room for doubt. In the seventh Maṇḍala, the seer of all the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-9-5; 7-25-11; 7-36-6; 7-40-3; 7-95; 7-96 is Vasiṣṭha. It is known that the hermitage of the seer Vasiṣṭha was near the hill of Abu (Mount Abu) of the Ārāvaḷī mountain in Rājapuṭānā. The first four mantras and the last two sūkta praise Sarasvatī, speaking of her as a river. Because the words सिंधुमाता (*siṃdhumātā*) and आसमुद्रात् (*āsamudrāt*) in these Ṛks apply especially to the river Sindhu, some say that it is the river Sindhu that is praised in these mantras. This dispute cannot be entirely set aside. Even so, it is not impossible that while speaking of one river another river is compared to it and a praise full of exaggeration is made. In the eighth Maṇḍala, for the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-21-17 and 18 the seer is Śobhari, son of the seer Kaṇva. In the Ṛks mentioned above this seer describes the sacrifice that a king named Citra performed on the bank of the river Sarasvatī, and the praise of that king's gifts. Since in the work Bṛhaddevatā of Śaunaka (6-58 to 62) it is said that this king Citra performed the sacrifice in Kurukṣetra, Sarasvatī must be a river nearby; and—
पूषा विष्णु र्हवनं मे सरस्वत्यवंतु सप्त सिंधवः । अपो वातः पर्वतासो वनस्पतिः शृणोतु पृथिवी हवं ॥ (*pūṣā viṣṇu rhavanaṃ me sarasvatyavaṃtu sapta siṃdhavaḥ | apo vātaḥ parvatāso vanaspatiḥ śṛṇotu pṛthivī havaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-54-9)
In this Ṛk, among the deities Pūṣā, Viṣṇu, Sarasvatī, Saptasindhavaḥ (seven rivers), Āpaḥ, Vātaḥ, Parvatāsaḥ, Vanaspatiḥ and Pṛthivī, Sarasvatī too is indicated. In the same way, in Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-97-32 the seer of that mantra, Vasiṣṭha or Pavitra, has used the word Sarasvatī. **NOTE**
### Printed p. 89
Here Sarasvatī may be either of two meanings, a goddess or a river. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-30-12 it is not stated definitely which river Sarasvatī is. In Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-64-9 the seer of that mantra, Gaya (a descendant of the seer Atri), has spoken of Sarasvatī together with the rivers Sindhu and Sarayū (Gogra); and—
इमं मे गंगे यमने सरस्वति शुतुद्रि स्तोमं सचता परुष्णा । असिक्ना मरुद्वधे वितस्तयार्जीकीये शृणुह्या सुषोमया ॥ (*imaṃ me gaṃge yamane sarasvati śutudri stomaṃ sacatā paruṣṇā | asiknā marudvadhe vitastayārjīkīye śṛṇuhyā suṣomayā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-75.5)
in this Ṛk the river Sarasvatī is spoken of together with the rivers Gaṅgā (the Ganges), Yamunā (the Jamna), Śutudri (the Sutlej), Paruṣṭhi (the Ravi), Asikki (the Chinab), Vitastā (the Jhelam), Marudvadhā (the United Stream of the Jhelam and Chenab), Arjikīyā (the Bias) and Suṣomā (the Indus); therefore it is clear that the river Sarasvatī is a separate river, and not the river Sindhu. The whole sūkta in which the above Ṛk stands is concerned with rivers. That is, for the nine Ṛks of this sūkta the rivers are the deities; the seer of this sūkta is Sindhuktit, son of the seer Priyamedha.
In the Āprī sūktas it is said that Sarasvatī is one of the three goddesses spoken of as तिस्रो देव्यः (*tisro devyaḥ*). In the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā there are ten sūktas called Āprī sūktas. In these sūktas तिस्रो देव्यः (*tisro devyaḥ*) means the praise of the three goddesses Iḷā, Sarasvatī and Bhāratī. The matter chiefly to be noted here is this: Agni is worshipped in three forms, on earth, in the atmosphere and in heaven. Since Bhāratī indicates the rays of the sun, this goddess (that is, the sun) is the form of Agni in heaven (the day). Since Sarasvatī is described in many places as the presiding deity of thunder and lightning, this Sarasvatī is the form of Agni in the atmosphere. Since the goddess Iḷā gives wealth and the like and long life to the people (the folk) on the earth, this goddess is the form of Agni on the earth; such is the indication of these Iḷā, Sarasvatī and Bhāratī. The seers have praised these three goddesses, asking that they come to the sacrificial altar and sit upon the spread of sacrificial grass (the strewn darbha grass).
We shall give some examples of the seers' praising Sarasvatī, thinking of her as a goddess other than the river.
(1) तान्पूर्वया निविदा हूमहे वयं भगं मित्रमदितिं दक्षमस्रिधं । अर्यमणं वरुणं सोममश्विना सरस्वती नः सुभगा मयस्करत् ॥ (*tānpūrvayā nividā hūmahe vayaṃ bhagaṃ mitramaditiṃ dakṣamasridhaṃ | aryamaṇaṃ varuṇaṃ somamaśvinā sarasvatī naḥ subhagā mayaskarat ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-89-3) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 90
In this Ṛk Sarasvatī is the goddess of speech or the presiding deity of thunder and lightning.
(2) सरस्वति त्वमस्मा अविड्डि मरुत्वती धृषती जेषि शत्रून् (*sarasvati tvamasmā aviḍḍi marutvatī dhṛṣatī jeṣi śatrūn*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-30-8) **GLOSS**
(3) सरस्वती शृणवन् यज्ञयासो धाता रयिं सहवीरं तुरासः (*sarasvatī śṛṇavan yajñayāso dhātā rayiṃ sahavīraṃ turāsaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-54-13) **GLOSS**
In these Ṛks Sarasvatī is thought of either as a river or as the presiding deity of thunder and lightning.
(4) दमूनसो अपसो ये सुहस्ता वृष्णः पत्निर्नद्यो विभ्वतष्टाः । सरस्वती बृहद्दिवोत राका दशस्यंतीर्वरिवस्यंतु शुभ्राः ॥ (*damūnaso apaso ye suhastā vṛṣṇaḥ patnirnadyo vibhvataṣṭāḥ | sarasvatī bṛhaddivota rākā daśasyaṃtīrvarivasyaṃtu śubhrāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-42-12) **GLOSS**
(5) आ नो दिवो बृहतः पर्वतादा सरस्वती यजता गंतु यज्ञं । (*ā no divo bṛhataḥ parvatādā sarasvatī yajatā gaṃtu yajñaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-43-11) **GLOSS**
(6) उभा नासत्या रुद्रो अध ग्नाः पूषा भगः सरस्वती जुषंत । (*ubhā nāsatyā rudro adha gnāḥ pūṣā bhagaḥ sarasvatī juṣaṃta |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-46-2) **GLOSS**
In all these three Ṛks Sarasvatī is the presiding deity of thunder and lightning. Griffith holds that in the first of these three Ṛks Sarasvatī is made out to be a river, either Sindhu or Sarasvatī, and in the Ṛks 2 and 3 a goddess. K. C. Caṭṭapādhyāya says that the Ṛks 1 and 2 apply to the river Sindhu. These three Ṛks were all uttered by descendants of the seer Atri. The seer of the first Ṛk is Atri or Bhauma, son of Atri; of the second Ṛk, Atri; of the third Ṛk, Pratikṣatra, a descendant of Atri. In the second Ṛk Sarasvatī is invoked from heaven (दिवः (*divaḥ*)) and from the cloud region (पर्वतादा (*parvatādā*)). Because the word पत्निः (*patniḥ*) occurs in the first Ṛk, the commentators have thought of Sarasvatī and the other rivers as the wives of Indra.
(7) अग्निरिंद्रो वरुणो मित्रो अर्यमा वायुः पूषा सरस्वती सजोषसः । (*agniriṃdro varuṇo mitro aryamā vāyuḥ pūṣā sarasvatī sajoṣasaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-65-1) **GLOSS**
(8) विश्वे देवासः शृणवन् वचांसि मे सरस्वती सह दीभिः पुरंध्या । (*viśve devāsaḥ śṛṇavan vacāṃsi me sarasvatī saha dībhiḥ puraṃdhyā |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-65-13) **GLOSS**
In both these Ṛks Sarasvatī is the goddess of speech.
### Printed p. 91
(9) यत्सुरामं व्यपिबः शचीभिः सरस्वती त्वा मघवन्नभिष्णुक् (*yatsurāmaṃ vyapibaḥ śacībhiḥ sarasvatī tvā maghavannabhiṣṇuk*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-131-5) **GLOSS**
Since in this Ṛk Sarasvatī is spoken of as being with Indra, Sarasvatī can be thought of either as the presiding deity of thunder and lightning or as the goddess of speech.
(10) गर्भं देहि सिनिवालि गर्भं धेहि सरस्वति (*garbhaṃ dehi sinivāli garbhaṃ dhehi sarasvati*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-184-2) **GLOSS**
Here it is fitting to call Sarasvatī the goddess of speech.
We shall describe as exactly as is known where the river Sarasvatī flowed in that age. This account is written following (1) R. D. Oldham in Journal of Asiatic Society Bengal 1886 page 322 (2) C F. Oldham, Journal of Royal Asiatic Society 1893, page 51 (3) Maps of the Cambridge History of India Vol. I Anciert India (4) Maps of Punjab published by the Surveyor-General of India (5) Ancient India as described by Magasthenesses and Arrian, these being the discourses.
In that age the river Sarasvatī was a large river. Rising in the Sismur (Sismur) mountain range in the Himalaya mountains, it flows westward and a little to the south. At its place of origin five small streams (small channels in which water flows) may have joined to form this river and flowed onward. Therefore the name Pañcajātā must have come to it. After flowing a little way, the river called Dṛṣadvatī (the modern Chitrang) comes flowing from the east and joins it. Apayā is a tributary of the Dṛṣadvatī. The Sarasvatī of that time lay to the south of the present river Sarasuti. (Rivers that flow in desert land often change their courses.) The small rivers Mārkaṇḍā (Markand) and Gaggar (Ghaggar) join the present river Sarasuti. Then Sarasvatī flows in the southern and western directions and at last joins the sea. The bed of the dried-up river now called Hakra (Hakra) must have been the bed of the Sarasvatī of that time. It may have joined with the present river (Sutlej) and become one with the river Sindhu. Or it may have gone and joined the river Sindhu directly. At that time the river Sindhu lay much farther east than it does now, and joined the sea near the gulf called (Rann of Kach). The scholar Max Müller (Max Muller) holds, on the basis of the word आसमुद्रात् (*āsamudrāt*) used in the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-95-2, that this river Sarasvatī went directly and joined the sea. Since before joining the sea this river flowed through a region of desert forest, the river may have sunk away in the desert before reaching the sea (See Muir's Sanskrit Texts Vol. II page 393). In this way
### Printed p. 92
the description of the river's sinking into the desert is given also in the Mahābhārata (Vana Parva, description of the pilgrimage to holy places) and in the Manusaṃhitā (1-21). For all these reasons it can be said that the present river Sarasuti must be the Sarasvatī of former times.
Above all other rivers the river Sarasvatī has the greatest pre-eminence. Although the idea of a goddess is very strong in this connection, the close kinship between the goddess and the rivers is always remembered. Three sūktas and many separate mantras are set apart for this river. Sarasvatī, Sarayū and Sindhu are great rivers [10-64-9]; Gaṅgā, Yamunā, Sarasvatī, Śutudrī, Paruṣṭi and some others, known and unknown, twenty-one rivers in all, are mentioned [10-75-5]. The kings and people on the bank of Sarasvatī are referred to [7-96.2; 8-21-18]. Sarasvatī is greater in glory than all other rivers; she has sap-filled water; she alone among all the rivers is wholly pure. She flows down from the ocean of the sky [compare 7-95.1 and 2, 5-43-11]. With her violent waves she sweeps away mountain peaks. Her unfathomable current roars and rushes with force [6-61.2 and 8]. Sarasvatī, greatest of the great and swiftest of the swift, is prayed to give her milk freely [6-61-13]. They have cried out that she should not carry them to new places [6-61.14]. She has seven sisters and she is sevenfold [6-61.10 and 12]. She is the mother of rivers; one of the seven rivers [7-36-6]; the mother most excellent among rivers and goddesses [2-41-16]. Her name is Pāvīravī [daughter of lightning] and she is the wife of a hero [6.49.7]. She occupies the regions of the earth, the wide sky and the three abodes [6-61-11 and 12]. There is an invitation to come to the sacrificial hall from the sky, from the great mountain [5-43-11]. From these last three statements birth in the world of heaven is indicated for Sarasvatī also, as is said of Gaṅgā in the Purāṇas and the like. In one place the expression Asuryā [goddess-woman] is used of her [7-96.1]. In the chariot of the Pitṛ gods, in their company, that goddess comes and takes her seat on the kuśa seat [10-17-8 and 9]; in the next two mantras too [10-17-10 and 11] the river is the object; therefore here too the river goddess must be what is meant. **NOTE**
She herself makes pure (1-3-10). She is called to come overflowing with streams (6-52-6); together with those waters that give wealth, offspring and immortality, she is prayed to grant vigour (10-30-12). She grants vigour and offspring (2-41-17) and is joined with the deities who help in obtaining offspring (10-184-2). She favoured Vadhvāśva with a son named Divodāsa
### Printed p. 93
(6-61-1). Her breasts, which never fail (compare Ai. Brā. 4-1), give every kind of wealth (1-164-49). She is the giver of wealth, prosperity and nourishment (7-95-2; 8-21-17; 9-67.32; 10-17-8 and 9) and is called Subhagā (the prosperous) (1 89.3; 7 95.4 and 6; 8-21 17). She, being a mother (Ambā), brings fame to the obscure (2.41.16); she makes devotion arise in her worshippers and increases it (1.3-10 and 11; 2-3.8; 6.61.4). She is invoked, together with the deities connected with praise (7-37-11; 10.65.13). She destroys those who revile the gods: she is fierce and a slayer of Vṛtra (6.61.3 and 7). But she protects those who worship her and destroys their enemies (7-95.4 and 5; 2.30.8; 9-49-7).
Sarasvatī is invoked together with many other deities. Apart from Pūṣan and Indra, it is especially along with the Marut gods that she is praised (3-54-13; 7-9-5; 7-39-5; 7-40-3). She is followed by them (2-30-8) or they are her friends (7-96-2). In one place she is joined also with the Aśvin gods. When the Aśvin gods helped Indra, Sarasvatī relieved his fatigue (10-131-5). When the gods performed a sacrifice, the Aśvin gods by their physician's art and Sarasvatī by her speech brought vigour to Indra (Vā. Saṃ. 19-12). Sarasvatī is the wife of the Aśvin gods (Vā. Saṃ. 19-94). In the Āprī sūkta, in the eighth and ninth Ṛks, she is many times joined with Iḷā and Bhāratī; the three together are thought of as a triad of deities; sometimes she is joined also with Mahī and Hotrā. This combination may indicate the purity of Sarasvatī. On the bank of Sarasvatī and Dṛṣṭadvatī Agni was kindled (3-23-4). The seers performed sacrifice on the bank of Sarasvatī (Ai. Brā. 2-19).
In the Ṛgveda Sarasvatī is a river goddess and no other quality is stated. But in the Brāhmaṇas Sarasvatī is the presiding deity of speech (Śa. Brā. 3-9-1-7; Ai. Brā. 3.1.10); in the Purāṇas and the like she is the presiding deity of knowledge and eloquence and the wife of Brahmā.
It cannot be said clearly which river the river Sarasvatī is. Some Western scholars hold that Sarasvatī must be a great river (probably the river Sindhu). Again, some say that it must have been a small river in the central region; that although it has now sunk into the sand and does not reach the sea, it was formerly a large river and reached the sea. Some say that Sarasvatī is a tributary of the Śutudri.
The greatness of rivers and the pre-eminence given to them by the seers can be learned from many sūktas. We give one of them here as an example. Concerning the dialogue that took place formerly between the seer Viśvāmitra and
### Printed p. 94
the rivers Vipāś and Śutudri, Sāyaṇa writes thus in the bhāṣya on the sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33—
पुरा किल विश्वामित्रः पैजवनस्य सुदासो राज्ञः पुरोहितो बभूव । स च पौरोहित्येन लब्धजनः सर्वं धनमादाय विपाट्टुतद्रोः संभेदमाययौ अनुययुरितरे । अथोत्ति तीर्षुर्विश्वामित्रः अगाधजले ते नद्यौ दृष्ट्वा उत्तरणार्थं आद्याभिः तिसृभिः तुष्टाव ॥ (*purā kila viśvāmitraḥ paijavanasya sudāso rājñaḥ purohito babhūva | sa ca paurohityena labdhajanaḥ sarvaṃ dhanamādāya vipāṭṭutadroḥ saṃbhedamāyayau anuyayuritare | athotti tīrṣurviśvāmitraḥ agādhajale te nadyau dṛṣṭvā uttaraṇārthaṃ ādyābhiḥ tisṛbhiḥ tuṣṭāva ‖*) (Sā. Bhā. Pīṭhikā 3-33) **NOTE**
Formerly Viśvāmitra was the family priest of the king named Sudāsa, son of Pijavana. By his priesthood he gained all the support of the people, took all the wealth, and reached the confluence of the rivers Vipāś and Śutudrī. Others also followed him. Then, seeing the rivers with very deep currents, the seer praised them with the first three mantras in order to cross them.
Thus he has stated the occasion of the story. These sentences tell the relation between Sudāsa and Viśvāmitra. Who this Sudāsa was, who Bharata was, what the relation between Sudāsa and Bharata was, and whether the priesthood common to both was Viśvāmitra's own, these and similar matters have to be examined here. To go on with that, we must first know the details of the conversation between Viśvāmitra and the rivers.
The two rivers Vipāś and Śutudrī, born from the lap-regions of the mountains, flow swiftly with their united currents, like horses that, having slipped their bridles and so with loosened bonds, run vying with one another, and like two cows running eagerly to approach their calves. (1)
O rivers, as a charioteer runs with his chariot to his goal, you two also, impelled by Indra and obeying Indra's command, go united towards the ocean. Flowing together and showing increase with your own waves, O shining rivers, one of you follows the other. (2)
I approach the river Vipāś, who has the utmost excess of motherly love. This river, wide and auspicious, like cows rushing swiftly to love their calf, goes and reaches, together with Śutudrī, a refuge common to both. (3)
The rivers: Making the earth fertile by this our current, according to the ordinance of the god Indra, we flow and go to our abode in the form of the ocean ordained by him
### Printed p. 95
and reach it. Following the path made by Indra we must not be late in going and reaching our destination. What may be the desire of this seer who prays to us? We wish to know it. (4)
Viśvāmitra: O rivers, filled with streams of water, check your current a little for me, who have set out to fetch Soma. That is my prayer. I, the son of Kuśika, desiring protection, with humility pray to the river Śutudrī that is before me. (5)
The rivers: When he slew the serpent that lay across all the flow of the waters, Indra, the bearer of the thunderbolt, made all the channels suited to our current. We flow subject to the command of Indra, who has an excellent hand and is the impeller of the world. (6)
Viśvāmitra: All the heroic deeds of Indra, who slew the asura Vṛtra with his thunderbolt, removed the obstruction that stood in the way of the free flow of the waters, and made them flow at will, deeds of extraordinary kind, are always worthy of praise. (7)
The rivers: O seer, praiser of Indra, do not forget the words of praise that you have now recited. By these very words this same praise will have to be performed in the ages to come. O seer, doer of good rites, serve us with these same words of praise in dialogue form. But do not deal with us only in a presumptuous way like human beings. Salutation to you. (8)
Viśvāmitra: O rivers, who are like my own kin, be gracious to me who praise you. I have come from a great distance, with my chariot and other burdens. To help me, reduce your currents a little. Make it convenient for me and my followers to cross. Regulate the current to a level a little below the rim of the wheel of our chariot. (9)
The rivers: O seer, praiser, we will grant your prayer. It is true that you have come from a far region with a chariot laden with burdens. We shall flow bowing before you. As a mother bows to lift up her darling child, and as a young woman bows to embrace her beloved, we shall flow bowing. (10)
Viśvāmitra: O rivers, you have made very many conveniences for crossing you easily. May the Bharatas who are with me also cross your united current. May the whole company that follows us, favoured by you and impelled by Indra, cross your current. We have become worthy of the favour of you who are worthy of our praise. After I have crossed you
### Printed p. 96
bring your current back to its former state. (11)
The Bharatas, who had set out in search of cows, crossed the rivers. The seer, having received the rivers' gracious regard, became satisfied. "O rivers, as distributors of the wealth of food and as increasers of that same wealth, carry your current onward. Filling your beds, flow with a full current." (12)
Even so, let the yoke-pin of our chariot be above the level of the current. Flow only to that extent. May the faultless and blameless Vipāś and Śutudri flow full. (13)
In this way the subject of this sūkta is that Viśvāmitra praised the rivers and crossed them.
In Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-53-9 the names of the rivers Rasā, Anitabhā, Kubhā and Sarayu are recited. Of these rivers, the names Anitabhā and Kubhā are recited in the Ṛgveda only in this Ṛk and are not mentioned anywhere else, so not much can be said about them. Perhaps these two rivers may be tributaries of some great river (Yamunā?). Therefore they are not so famous.
Sarayū—The river called Sarayū is famous in the Purāṇas. In the Rāmāyaṇa there is a description that the city of Ayodhyā stood on the bank of the river Sarayū. In the Ṛgveda also the name of this river occurs in the present Ṛk, and in the Ṛk
उत त्या सद्य आर्या सरयोरिंद्र पारतः । अर्णाचित्ररथावधीः । (*uta tyā sadya āryā sarayoriṃdra pārataḥ | arṇācitrarathāvadhīḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-30-18)
it is stated that Indra killed a king named Citraratha on the bank of the river called Sarayu.
सरस्वती सरयुः सिंधुरूर्मिभिर्महो महीरवसा यंतु वक्षणीः (*sarasvatī sarayuḥ siṃdhurūrmibhirmaho mahīravasā yaṃtu vakṣaṇīḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-64-9)
In this Ṛk there is a prayer that the rivers Sarasvatī, Sarayu and Sindhu may come with their protections. The river Sarayu is spoken of in the Ṛgveda only in these three Ṛks. Nowhere else.
Rasā—It must be a famous river, or there is reason also to suppose that this name was borne by the river Sindhu, which is famous now. It was famous as a divine river, and once upon a time, when the water of this river dried up for some reason, the Aśvin gods by their power

### Printed p. 97
filled with water. This history is stated in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-112-12, in the Ṛk where the qualities of the Aśvin gods are described.
अत्रेतिहासमाचक्षते । पणिभिरसुरैरपह्रियमाणा गा अनुगच्छंतः देवगोपाला रसां नामांतरिक्षनदीमगाधोदकां नोत्तरितुं शेकुः । तद्ज्ञात्वा देवाः संक्रुद्धाः रसां शेपुः निरुदका भवेति । सा प्रत्यनीतासु गोषु अश्विनौ परिचचार । तामश्विन् गत्वा पुनरगाधोदकां चक्रुतुरिति ॥ (*atretihāsamācakṣate | paṇibhirasurairapahriyamāṇā gā anugacchaṃtaḥ devagopālā rasāṃ nāmāṃtarikṣanadīmagādhodakāṃ nottarituṃ śekuḥ | tadjñātvā devāḥ saṃkruddhāḥ rasāṃ śepuḥ nirudakā bhaveti | sā pratyanītāsu goṣu aśvinau paricacāra | tāmaśvin gatvā punaragādhodakāṃ cakruturiti ‖*)
The Paṇis, who are asuras, carried off the cows of the gods. The divine herdsmen followed that path in pursuit of the asuras, but on the way a river named Rasā, of the atmosphere, with an unfathomable flow of water, lay across their path, and they were not able to cross it. When the gods came to know this, they were angry and cursed that river that it should become dry. Immediately all the water of that river dried up. After the cows had been driven back, the river Rasā prayed to the Aśvin gods; they came and filled that river with abundant water. Skandasvāmin has narrated this story in his commentary. Sāyaṇa's explanation differs a little. His explanation is—
रसा नदी भवति रसतेः शब्दकर्मण इति यास्कः । नि. ११-२५ । हे अश्विनौ याभिरूतिभिर्हेतुभूताभी रसां नदीमनावृष्ट्या जलरहितां क्षोदसा कूलानि संपिंषतोद्न उदकेन पिपिस्वथु: युवां पूरितवंतौ ॥ (*rasā nadī bhavati rasateḥ śabdakarmaṇa iti yāskaḥ | ni. 11-25 | he aśvinau yābhirūtibhirhetubhūtābhī rasāṃ nadīmanāvṛṣṭyā jalarahitāṃ kṣodasā kūlāni saṃpiṃṣatodna udakena pipisvathu: yuvāṃ pūritavaṃtau ‖*) (Sā. Bhā.) **NOTE**
Having cited the derivation given by the author of the Nirukta, that the word Rasā is formed from the root रस् (*ras*) which has the sense "make a sound", he says that the water of a river flows swiftly and therefore makes a noise, and for that reason this river is called Rasā. But he has given absence of rain as the reason for the drying up of the water of this river. In some places it is said that the water of this river has an unfathomable current and is impossible to cross.
कास्मेहितिः का परितक्का सीत्कथं रसाया आतरः पयांसि । (*kāsmehitiḥ kā paritakkā sītkathaṃ rasāyā ātaraḥ payāṃsi |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-108-1)
Formerly the Paṇis carried off the cows of the gods and hid them in the cave of a mountain, so that no one knew. At the prayer of the gods, Indra sent Saramā, the divine dog (the female dog of the world of the gods), to discover the place where the cows were hidden. She travelled a very great distance, crossed forests and wildernesses, passed over many rivers, and at last came to the place where the Paṇis were.
### Printed p. 98
Then a charming conversation took place between the Paṇis and Saramā on the matter of giving the cows back to the gods. The Paṇis, questioning Saramā, ask how she crossed the river Rasā, with its unfathomable flood of water, when she came here. Therefore it may be said that the river Rasā was filled with an unfathomable flood of water. Besides these passages, in
सिषक्तु माता मही रसा नः स्मत्सूरिभिर्यजुहस्त ऋजुवनिः । (*siṣaktu mātā mahī rasā naḥ smatsūribhiryajuhasta ṛjuvaniḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 54-41-15) **NOTE**
the word Rasā is used. When explaining this Ṛk Sāyaṇa has said रसा सारभूता भूमिः (*rasā sārabhūtā bhūmiḥ*). But since this Ṛk has the word सिषक्तु (*siṣaktu*), "may she moisten", there is room for the surmise that the word Rasā here is specifically the river named Rasā. The name of the river Rasā is also indicated in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-43-6; 10-75-6; 10-121-4; 5-53-9; 7-72-13, and so on.
The seventy-fifth sūkta.

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र सु व इति नवर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं । प्रियमेधपुत्रस्य सिंधुक्षित आर्षं
> जागतं नदीदेवताकं । तथा चानुक्रांतं । प्र सु नव सिंधुक्षित्र्ययमेधो
> नदीस्तुतिर्जागतं श्विति ॥ गतो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning प्र सु व (*pra su va*) has nine Ṛks and is the seventh sūkta. It is the work of the seer Sindhukṣit, son of Priyamedha, its metre is Jagatī, and its deity is the rivers. So it is recorded in the Anukramaṇī: प्र सु नव सिंधुक्षित्र्ययमेधो नदीस्तुतिर्जागतं श्विति (*pra su nava siṃdhukṣitryayamedho nadīstutirjāgataṃ śviti*) **NOTE**. The use in the ritual has been stated (गतो विनियोगः (*gato viniyogaḥ*)).
Anuvāda: This sūkta beginning प्र सु व (*pra su va*) is the seventh sūkta in the sixth Anuvāka. It has nine Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Sindhukṣit, the son of the seer Priyamedha; the deity is the rivers; the metre is Jagatī. In the Anukramaṇī it is said प्र सु नव सिंधुक्षित्मेधो नदीस्तुतिर्जागतं तु (*pra su nava siṃdhukṣitmedho nadīstutirjāgataṃ tu*). The use in the ritual has been stated before.
Sūkta 75.
Maṇḍala 10; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 75.
Aṣṭaka 8; Adhyāya 3; Varga 6, 7.
Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9.
Seer: सिंधुक्षितैयमेधः (*siṃdhukṣitaiyamedhaḥ*).
Deity: पद्यः (*padyaḥ*) **NOTE**.
Metre: जगती (*jagatī*).
### Printed p. 99
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र सु व॑ आपो महि॒मान॑मुत्त॒मं का॒रुर्वो॑चाति॒ सद॑ने  ॒॑
> वि॒वस्व॑तः ।  
> प्र स॒प्तस॑प्त त्रे॒धा हि च॑क्र॒मुः प्र सृत्व॑रीणा॒मति॒ सिंधु॒रोज॑सा॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । सु । वः । आपः । महिमानं । उत्तमं । कारुः । वोचाति । सदने । विवस्वतः ।  
> प्र । सप्तसप्त । त्रेधा । हि । चक्रमुः । प्र । सृत्वरीणां । अति । सिंधुः । ओजसा ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे आपः वो युष्माकं महिमानं महत्त्वं स्तोत्रमत्तममुत्कृष्टतमं
> कारुः स्तोता सिंधुक्षिदहं विवस्वतः परिचरणवतो यजमानस्य सदने यज्ञगृहे
> सु सुष्टु प्र वोचाति । प्रब्रवीमि। ता नद्यः सप्तसप्त भूत्वा त्रेधा पृथिव्यामंतरिक्षे
> दिवि चेति त्रिधा त्रिःप्रकारं चक्रमुः । प्रावहन् । सृत्वरीणामासां मध्यॆ सिंधुरेतन्नामिका नद्योजसा स्वबलेनाति सर्वा अपि नदीरतिक्रम्य प्रवहतीति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O waters, the praiser, Sindhukṣit, utters your greatness, that is, your majesty, as the most excellent praise, well (सु (*su*)) and fully (प्र वोचाति (*pra vocāti*), glossed प्रब्रवीमि (*prabravīmi*), "I proclaim"), in the dwelling, the sacrificial house, of Vivasvat, that is, of the institutor of the rite, who has attendants. Those rivers, becoming sevens each (सप्तसप्त (*saptasapta*)), flowed in three ways (त्रेधा (*tredhā*), glossed त्रिःप्रकारं (*triḥprakāraṃ*)), that is, on the earth, in the atmosphere and in heaven; चक्रमुः (*cakramuḥ*) is glossed प्रावहन् (*prāvahan*), "they flowed forth". Among these flowing rivers (सृत्वरीणां (*sṛtvarīṇāṃ*)) the river named Sindhu, by its strength (ओजसा (*ojasā*), glossed स्वबलेन (*svabalena*)), surpasses all the other rivers and flows on; the verb "flows on" is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आपः (*āpaḥ*)—O waters
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- महिमानं (*mahimānaṃ*)—greatness
- उत्तमं (*uttamaṃ*)—exceedingly
- कारुः (*kāruḥ*)—the praiser
- विवस्वतः (*vivasvataḥ*)—of the institutor of the rite
- सदने (*sadane*)—in the sacrificial house
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- प्रवोचाति (*pravocāti*)—praises (those rivers)
- सप्तसप्त (*saptasapta*)—in sevens
- त्रेधा (*tredhā*)—in the three worlds beginning with the earth
- त्रेधा (*tredhā*)—in three ways
- चक्रमुः (*cakramuḥ*)—they flow
- सृत्वरीणां (*sṛtvarīṇāṃ*)—among these flowing rivers
- सिंधुः (*siṃdhuḥ*)—the Sindhu
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—by its own greatness
- अति (*ati*)—surpassing everything
- प्र (वहति) (*pra (vahati)*)—flows forth

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O waters, the praiser exceedingly praises your greatness in the house of the institutor of the rite. You break up into sevens and flow in three ways in the three worlds beginning with the earth. Among the flowing rivers the Sindhu, by its own greatness, surpasses all the rest.
### Printed p. 100

##### English translation
Waters, the worshipper addresses to you excellent praise in the dwelling of the institutor of the rite; they flowed by sevens through the three (worlds); but the Sindhu surpasses (all) the (other) streams in strength.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सप्त सप्त (*sapta sapta*)—in the form of seven and seven rivers; the waters that fall from the atmosphere upon the earth as rain flow as (the principal) seven rivers and join the ocean. Of those seven rivers the river called Sindhu is the greatest of all the other rivers, that is, in the quantity of its water, in its breadth, in its length and in its speed; so the seer has described. Like these seven rivers on the earth, there are also seven rivers each in the atmosphere (the middle region) and in heaven (the names of these seven chief rivers are recited in the fifth Ṛk of this sūkta); this the seer made known by the words सप्त सप्त श्रेधा (*sapta sapta średhā*). But how the division into seven rivers applies to the waters that exist in the atmosphere and in heaven in the form of clouds is not clear. Perhaps the idea suggested may be that, since all the waters of the seven rivers on the earth exist in heaven and the atmosphere and fall from there upon the earth, these seven rivers (or their waters) were as if in heaven and the atmosphere.
