#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्व॑कर्मन्ह॒विषा॑ वावृधा॒नः स्व॒यं य॑जस्व पृथि॒वीमु॒त द्यां ।  
> मुह्यं॑त्व॒न्ये अ॒भितो॒ जना॑स इ॒हास्माकं॑ म॒घवा॑ सू॒रिर॑स्तु ॥६॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वकर्मन् । हविषा । ववृधानः । स्वयं । यजस्व । पृथिवीं । उत । द्यां ।  
> मुह्यंतु । अन्ये । अभितः । जनासः । इह । अस्माकं । मघवा । सूरिः । अस्तु ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> तस्मिन्नेव वैश्वकर्मणपशौ वपापुरोडाशयोर्विश्वकर्मन्हविषेति क्रमेण
> द्वे अनुवाक्ये । सूत्रितं च । विश्वकर्मन्हविषा नावृधान इति द्वे विश्वकर्मा विमना
> आद्विहायाः । आ. ३-८ । इति ॥ साकमेधेषु वैश्वकर्मणस्य विश्वकर्मन्हविषेत्येष्टैवानुवाक्या । सूत्रं तु पूर्वमुदाहृतं ॥
> हे विश्वकर्मन्विश्वविषयकर्मन्नेतन्नामक परमेश्वर हविषा हविर्भूतेन
> मया विश्वकर्मणा मया दत्तेन वा हविषा ववृधानो वर्धमानः । विश्वकर्मा
> सर्वाणि भूतानि जुहवांचकार स आत्मानमप्यंततो जुहवांचकारेति हि निरुक्तं
> पूर्वमुदाहृतं । स्वयमेव पृथिवीमुतापि च द्यां दिवं च स्वसृष्टे द्यावापृथिव्यौ स्वयं प्रवृद्धः सन्यजस्व । पूजय । अन्ये मत्तोऽन्ये जनासो जना
> अयष्टारोऽस्मद्यागनिरोधिनो वा मुह्यंतु मुग्धा भवंत्वभितः सर्वतः । अथ
> परोक्षकृतः। इहास्मिन्यागेऽस्माकं मघवास्मत्पत्तेन हविर्लक्षणेन धनेन धनवान् स
> सूरिः स्वर्गादिफलस्य प्रेरकोऽस्तु । भवतु । अत्र विश्वकर्मन्हविषा वर्धमान इत्यादि
> निरुक्तमनुसंधेयं । १०-२७ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the animal offering to Viśvakarman, two anuvākyā verses are used in order for the vapā and the cake, beginning with this Ṛk; the sūtra (Ā. 3-8) is cited. In the Sākamedha offerings this Ṛk alone is the anuvākyā, and the sūtra has been cited before. O Viśvakarman, Supreme Lord of that name, whose work is the universe, increased by the oblation, whether by the oblation that is myself or by the oblation given by me, worship by yourself the earth and the heaven, your own creations, grown great. Let other people, who do not sacrifice or who obstruct our sacrifice, be bewildered on every side. Then, in the indirect form: in this sacrifice may Maghavan, made rich by the wealth in the form of the oblation provided by us, be the impeller to the fruit of heaven and the rest. Here the Nirukta is to be considered: विश्वकर्मा सर्वाणि भूतानि जुहवांचकार स आत्मानमप्यंततो जुहवांचकार (*viśvakarmā sarvāṇi bhūtāni juhavāṃcakāra sa ātmānamapyaṃtato juhavāṃcakāra*), cited earlier (10-27).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विश्वकर्मन् (*viśvakarman*)—O Viśvakarman
- हविषा (*haviṣā*)—with the oblation
- ववृधानः (*vavṛdhānaḥ*)—having grown
- स्वयं (*svayaṃ*)—yourself
- पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*)—the earth
- उत (*uta*)—and
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the heavenly world
- यजस्व (*yajasva*)—worship
- अन्ये जनासः (*anye janāsaḥ*)—the other people, devoid of rites
- अभितः (*abhitaḥ*)—everywhere
- मुह्यंतु (*muhyaṃtu*)—let them be overcome by delusion
- मघवा (*maghavā*)—the Lord, the possessor of wealth
- इह (*iha*)—in this our sacrifice
- अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*)—for us
- सूरिः (*sūriḥ*)—as the impeller to heaven and the rest
- अस्तु (*astu*)—let him be

### Printed p. 225

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Viśvakarman, increased by our oblation, do you yourself worship heaven and earth. Let the other people, who are devoid of rites, be overcome everywhere by delusion. May Viśvakarman, the possessor of wealth, be in this sacrifice of ours the impeller of us to heaven.

##### English translation
Vishwakarman, magnified by the oblation, do you of yourself worship earth and heaven; let other men (who offer no oblations) be everywhere confounded; may he, rich in oblations, be the granter of heaven at this our (sacrifice).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

For this Ṛk Yāska has given the following explanation:

विश्वकर्मन् हविषा वर्धयमानः स्वयं यजस्व पृथिवीं च दिवं च मुह्यंत्वन्ये अभितो जनाः सपत्ना इहास्माकं मघवा सूरिरस्तु प्रज्ञाता॥ (*viśvakarman haviṣā vardhayamānaḥ svayaṃ yajasva pṛthivīṃ ca divaṃ ca muhyaṃtvanye abhito janāḥ sapatnā ihāsmākaṃ maghavā sūrirastu prajñātā‖*) (Ni. 10-27)

In this Ṛk the action performed by Viśvakarman and the fruit obtained by it are praised. O Viśvakarman, you who grow by the oblation offered in the sacrifice called Sarvamedha, offer the earth and heaven in the sacrifice; that is, yourself pervade and abide in heaven and earth. Let the other people, that is, all your rivals who do not worship you, be without discernment and be overcome by delusion. Thereafter may the Lord of all (Viśvakarman), the master of all wealth and endowed with unobstructed knowledge, be the impeller of us in this sacrificial rite, or in the rite that is the worship of Viśvakarman.

सपत्नाः (*sapatnāḥ*): ये समानं पतित्वमिच्छंति ते सपत्नाः (*ye samānaṃ patitvamicchaṃti te sapatnāḥ*). Those who each wish for lordship for themselves, that is, those who fight for equal authority, rivals, enemies: this is the meaning.

In the animal offering made to the deity Viśvakarman, when the vapā and the cake are offered, this Ṛk and the next Ṛk are to be recited as anuvākyā mantras; this is explained by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra in the sūtra विश्वकर्मन् हविषा नावृधान इति द्वे विश्वकर्मा विमना अद्विहायाः (*viśvakarman haviṣā nāvṛdhāna iti dve viśvakarmā vimanā advihāyāḥ*) (Ā. 3-8), and

### Printed p. 226

in the Sākamedha offerings too, the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra sentence which says that this Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra on behalf of the deity Viśvakarman has been cited earlier.

The praise of Viśvakarman, who is made of sacrifice and is the root of sacrifice, is the special subject of this sūkta. Viśvakarman is the performer of the sacrifice and the enjoyer of the sacrifice. Before creation he himself is the place of sacrifice; after creation the universe itself is the place of sacrifice. Creation is the manifestation of the subtle Viśvakarman in gross form. For this creation too, sacrifice is the root. In this sacrifice the oblation is Viśvakarman and the enjoyer of the oblation is also he. The expansive manifestation of himself in the form of the universe is the fruit of the sacrifice. That is what is stated in the present Ṛk as हविषा वावृधानः (*haviṣā vāvṛdhānaḥ*).

This is a very profound matter. It contains spiritual and divine principles. The creation, preservation and dissolution of the universe are all in the form of a cycle. All are only particular states of the Lord. All of this is made of sacrifice. Sacrifice is the root of everything; everything is of the form of sacrifice. The sacrifice of the form of creation, preservation and dissolution must go on continuously. These are particular states that are of the nature of motion. This point is understood from the very etymology of the word yajña.

अथ यस्माद्यज्ञो नाम । स्तंति वा एनमेतद्यदभिषुण्वंति तद्यदेनं तन्नते तदेनं जनयंति स तायमानो जायते स यन् जायते तस्माद्यज्ञो यंजो ह वै नामैतद्यद्यज्ञ इति ॥ (*atha yasmādyajño nāma | staṃti vā enametadyadabhiṣuṇvaṃti tadyadenaṃ tannate tadenaṃ janayaṃti sa tāyamāno jāyate sa yan jāyate tasmādyajño yaṃjo ha vai nāmaitadyadyajña iti ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 3-9-4-3)

When the Soma is pressed in the sacrifice, it is as if the sacrifice is killed.**NOTE** When they make the sacrifice spread out, then there is its birth again. Because there is thus an expansion and a contraction of the nature of motion, (यन् जायते (*yan jāyate*)) it is called yajña. Yajña is yajña.

सर्वेषां वा एष भूतानाग्ं सर्वेषां देवानामात्मा यद्यज्ञस्तस्य समृद्धिमनु यजमानः प्रजया पशुभिर्युध्यते ॥ (*sarveṣāṃ vā eṣa bhūtānāgṃ sarveṣāṃ devānāmātmā yadyajñastasya samṛddhimanu yajamānaḥ prajayā paśubhiryudhyate ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 14-3-2-1)

For all the gods and men and the rest this sacrifice is the self. The sacrificer who performs this sacrifice obtains all prosperity and increase: thus the special excellence of the sacrifice is praised. Just as Viśvakarman is the self of all the gods and men and the rest, so the sacrifice too is of the nature of all. The two are not different: Viśvakarman is the sacrifice, and the sacrifice is of the nature of Viśvakarman. The sacrifice is the reflection of Prajāpati, who is of the form of Viśvakarman.

### Printed p. 227

स देवेभ्य आत्मानं प्रदाय । अथैतमात्मनः प्रतिमामसृजत (*sa devebhya ātmānaṃ pradāya | athaitamātmanaḥ pratimāmasṛjata*) (Śa. Brā. 11-1-8-3)

After Prajāpati had offered himself to the gods, he created the reflection of himself in the form of the sacrifice. "Because he created a reflection of himself in the form of the sacrifice, the knowers say that Prajāpati is of the form of the sacrifice": such is the sentence that teaches identity. As Prajāpati (or Viśvakarman) is described in the Ṛks above in this same sūkta as being of the nature of the universe, in the same way the sacrifice, which is the reflection of Prajāpati, is also so. In the section on हविषा वावृधानः (*haviṣā vāvṛdhānaḥ*) the aspect of the growth of Prajāpati has been indicated. It was explained above that Prajāpati, in the form of Viśvakarman, is expanded in the forms of heaven, earth and atmosphere, and in the forms of Agni, Vāyu and the sun. The sacrifice, which is his reflection, is also of the nature of the universe. After the identity of Agni and the sacrifice has been stated, it is shown that the sacrificial altar, which reflects the sacrifice, pervades the universe, and that all the masses of light in the universe and the other beings, moving and unmoving, are its limbs.

अयं वाव लोक एषोग्निश्चितः । तस्याप एव परिश्रितो मनुष्या यजुष्मत्यः ॥ अंतरिक्षग्ं ह त्वैवैषोऽग्निश्चितः । तस्य द्यावापृथिव्योरेव संधिः............ परिश्रितः परेण हांतरिक्षं........ ॥ दौर्ह त्वेवैषोग्निश्चितः । तस्याप एव परिश्रितो यथा ह वा इदं कोशः समुच्चित एवमिमे लोका अप्स्वंतः.................. ॥ (*ayaṃ vāva loka eṣogniścitaḥ | tasyāpa eva pariśrito manuṣyā yajuṣmatyaḥ ‖ aṃtarikṣagṃ ha tvaivaiṣo 'gniścitaḥ | tasya dyāvāpṛthivyoreva saṃdhiḥ............ pariśritaḥ pareṇa hāṃtarikṣaṃ........ ‖ daurha tvevaiṣogniścitaḥ | tasyāpa eva pariśrito yathā ha vā idaṃ kośaḥ samuccita evamime lokā apsvaṃtaḥ.................. ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 10-5-4-1-3)

This sacrificial altar, which reflects the sacrifice, is the terrestrial world. The bricks that surround it are the oceans. The yajuṣmatī bricks are men, the sandoha bricks are the animals. The lokaṃpṛṇā is Agni. One who knows this principle obtains lordship over the worlds.

This sacrificial altar, which reflects the sacrifice, is the atmosphere. Since the junction of heaven and earth lies in the region beyond the atmosphere, this altar is surrounded; the junction of heaven and earth is in the yajuṣmatī bricks, which are the birds. The clay between the bricks is the rays. The oblations are the fuel sticks; the lokaṃpṛṇā is Vāyu. One who knows this principle attains identity with Agni.

### Printed p. 228

This sacrificial altar is the heavenly world. The surrounding bricks are the oceans that encircle this world. The bricks called yajuṣmatī are the gods. The sūdadoha bricks are the food of this world. The clay between the bricks is the stars.

आदित्यो ह त्वैवैषोऽग्निश्चितः । तस्य दिश एव परिश्रितस्ताः षष्टिश्च त्रीणि च शतानि भवंति षष्टिश्च ह वै त्रीणि च शतान्यादित्यं दिशः समंतं परियंति ............ ॥ नक्षत्राणि ह त्वैवैषोऽग्निश्चितः । तानि वा एतानि सप्तविग्ंशतिर्नक्षत्राणि........ ॥ सर्वाणि ह त्वेव भूतानि । सर्वे देवा एषोऽग्निश्चित आपो वै सर्वे देवाः सर्वाणि भूतानि ता हैता अप एवैषोऽग्निश्चितः......... आत्मायमेव सोऽयं हिरण्मयः पुरुषः ॥ (*ādityo ha tvaivaiṣo 'gniścitaḥ | tasya diśa eva pariśritastāḥ ṣaṣṭiśca trīṇi ca śatāni bhavaṃti ṣaṣṭiśca ha vai trīṇi ca śatānyādityaṃ diśaḥ samaṃtaṃ pariyaṃti ............ ‖ nakṣatrāṇi ha tvaivaiṣo 'gniścitaḥ | tāni vā etāni saptavigṃśatirnakṣatrāṇi........ ‖ sarvāṇi ha tveva bhūtāni | sarve devā eṣo 'gniścita āpo vai sarve devāḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni tā haitā apa evaiṣo 'gniścitaḥ......... ātmāyameva so 'yaṃ hiraṇmayaḥ puruṣaḥ ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 10-5-4-6 to 14)

This sacrificial altar, the reflection of the sacrifice, is the sun. Three hundred and sixty directions and intermediate directions surround the sun. The three hundred and sixty yajuṣmatī bricks are the three hundred and sixty rays of the sun. The sūdadoha bricks are the special principles that stand between the rays of the sun and the directions. This sacrificial altar is all the stars. The seven metres. This is the year, and this is all beings. Just as three hundred and sixty boats in the form of rays surround the sun, so around this altar the gods, men and the rest, in the form of the yajuṣmatī bricks, stand around. The body of this altar is the golden person who is within the sun. Thus it is shown that Viśvakarman (or Prajāpati), who is of the nature of the universe, and the sacrifice are the doer and the deed, reflecting one and the same principle. How attractive and how great is the nature of this golden person, who is the body of the sacrificial altar? It is this:

तस्यैते प्रतिष्ठे । रुक्ष्मश्च पुष्करणपर्णं चापश्चादित्यमंडलं च स्रुचौ बाहू ताविंद्राग्नी द्वे स्वयमातृण्णे इयं चांतरिक्षं च तिस्रो विश्वज्योतिष एता देवता अग्निर्वायुरादित्य एता ह्येव देवता विश्वं ज्योतिः................। (*tasyaite pratiṣṭhe | rukṣmaśca puṣkaraṇaparṇaṃ cāpaścādityamaṃḍalaṃ ca srucau bāhū tāviṃdrāgnī dve svayamātṛṇṇe iyaṃ cāṃtarikṣaṃ ca tisro viśvajyotiṣa etā devatā agnirvāyurāditya etā hyeva devatā viśvaṃ jyotiḥ................|*) (Śa. Brā. 18-5-42)

The golden vessel and the lotus leaf are the two feet of this golden person who is within the sun. The two ladles on the sacrificial altar are his arms. They are Indra and Agni. These two bricks, naturally perforated, are the earth and the atmosphere. The bricks in the form of the light of the universe are all the deities. Their number is three. These three reflect Agni, Vāyu and the sun.

### Printed p. 229

The body of this altar is the end of all. This golden person within the sun, who is of the form of the altar, having obtained all desires, is free from desire and perfectly satisfied. In this way identity is shown between Prajāpati, who is the self of the universe and Viśvakarman, and the sacrifice, which pervades the whole universe and sustains it. This Prajāpati, offering himself as the oblation in his own sacrifice, resolves on the work of creation and expands the world.

य आत्मदा बलदा यस्य विश्व उपासते प्रशिषं यस्य देवाः । यस्य छायामृतं यस्य मृत्युः कस्मै देवाय हविषा विधाम ॥ (*ya ātmadā baladā yasya viśva upāsate praśiṣaṃ yasya devāḥ | yasya chāyāmṛtaṃ yasya mṛtyuḥ kasmai devāya haviṣā vidhāma ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10.121-2)

It is described that this Prajāpati, called Ka, at the time of creation is the giver of life to all beings (or the one who offers himself in the form of the oblation), the giver of strength, and that the gods too obey his command, and so on. The meaning is that this Prajāpati offered himself in his own universal sacrifice and brought the work of creation to fulfilment.

Just as Viśvakarman, who pervades the universe, is the cause of creation, preservation and dissolution, so the sacrifice, which is his action following his resolve and pervades the universe, is also the root cause of creation, preservation and dissolution.

Earlier, in explaining the purport of the Ṛk यज्ञेन यज्ञमयजंत देवाः (*yajñena yajñamayajaṃta devāḥ*) (1-164-50), it was stated that this sacrifice is the root of all the worldly laws. The laws of this sacrifice, established in the atmosphere, keep the gods who dwell in the heavenly world in their respective places. Through the sacrifice all beings created by Prajāpati obtain the food and strength that are the cause of their continuance.

The prayer in the form of a wish, इहास्माकं मघवा सूरिरस्तु (*ihāsmākaṃ maghavā sūrirastu*), shows the need for prosperity both worldly and of the other world. Whatever means and resources are indispensable and desired for attaining excellent growth in the period of preservation after creation, all that is prayed for here. After creation, the first thing needed is food. For obtaining this food the sacrifice is the main means.

प्रजापतिः प्रजा असृजत तास्सृष्टाः क्षुधं न्यायन्त्स एतं नवरात्रमपश्यत्तमाहरत्तेनायजत ततो पै प्रजाभ्योकल्पत यर्हि प्रजाः क्षुधं निगच्छेयुस्तर्हि नवरात्रेण यजतेमे हि ना एतासां लोका अक्षृप्ता अथैकाः क्षुधं निगच्छंति ॥ (*prajāpatiḥ prajā asṛjata tāssṛṣṭāḥ kṣudhaṃ nyāyantsa etaṃ navarātramapaśyattamāharattenāyajata tato pai prajābhyokalpata yarhi prajāḥ kṣudhaṃ nigaccheyustarhi navarātreṇa yajateme hi nā etāsāṃ lokā akṣṛptā athaikāḥ kṣudhaṃ nigacchaṃti ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 7-2-4-1)

### Printed p. 230

All the creatures produced by Prajāpati, the cause of the world, were filled with hunger. Then, to remove the hunger, he thought of the sacrifice of the form of Navarātra and performed the sacrifice by it. It removed the hunger of the creatures. Therefore the sacrifice is the root for removing hunger.

This same sacrifice keeps the three worlds in one state and under law. Just as a cloth is made by joining thread with thread in warp and woof, so the sacrifice became the root for preventing looseness, for producing one power of the form of a single connection for the whole universe, and for the three worlds being in their ordained state.

But the activity of the universe does not end here. As soon as Viśvakarman had produced the creation rooted in sacrifice, the principle that was in unity attained plurality. This plurality too must attain growth. Just as Viśvakarman, who is of the form of Prajāpati, performed sacrifice for his own growth, so these many produced beings too must perform sacrifice for their growth. They must follow the same path that Viśvakarman showed. Just as he worshipped himself in the form of his sacrifice, so every creature must worship that same Viśvakarman through the sacrifice, which is the reflection of Viśvakarman. If the growth of the produced universe is desired, then as long as one is in the period of preservation, the performance of sacrifice must be the chief aim of life. Sacrifice is the duty of the man who is born.

ऋणग्ं ह वै जायते योऽस्ति स जायमान एव देवेभ्य ऋषिभ्यः पित्ृभ्यो मनुष्येभ्यः ॥ (*ṛṇagṃ ha vai jāyate yo 'sti sa jāyamāna eva devebhya ṛṣibhyaḥ pitṛbhyo manuṣyebhyaḥ ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 1-7-2-1)

Quoting such sentences, the matter of man's threefold debt has been explained earlier. In order that the creature, freed from this debt, may keep up his existence so as to take part in the continuance of the universe, every individual must perform this sacrifice, which is the reflection of Viśvakarman.

What is the nature of the growth of Viśvakarman in the sentence हविषा वर्धमानः (*haviṣā vardhamānaḥ*), and what is its course? This is to be examined here.

प्रजापतिः प्रजा असृजत । तास्सृष्टास्समश्लिष्यन् । ता रूपेणानु प्राविशत् । तस्मादाहुः । रूपं वै प्रजापतिरिति । ता नाम्नानुप्राविशत् । तस्मादाहुः । नाम वै प्रजापतिरिति ॥ (*prajāpatiḥ prajā asṛjata | tāssṛṣṭāssamaśliṣyan | tā rūpeṇānu prāviśat | tasmādāhuḥ | rūpaṃ vai prajāpatiriti | tā nāmnānuprāviśat | tasmādāhuḥ | nāma vai prajāpatiriti ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 2-2-7-1)

### Printed p. 231

The creatures created by Prajāpati did not attain an individual state; all of them remained joined together. Then, in order to separate them individually, he gave them name and form, divided those that were together by these names and forms, and himself too entered into them. These names and forms became the root of the expansion of the world.

Just as the sacrifice is the means of Viśvakarman's growth, so also this same sacrifice is the means of growth of the individual creature, divided by name and form. Just as unity between Viśvakarman and the sacrifice has been shown, so there must be identity between the individual creature and the sacrifice performed by him. Just as Viśvakarman offers himself as the oblation (स्वयं यजस्व तन्वं वृधानः (*svayaṃ yajasva tanvaṃ vṛdhānaḥ*)), so the creature created by him must also offer himself, in the form of an oblation, in this individual sacrifice. How? —

अथ यद्यज्ञं तनुते । यज्ञेनैवैतद्देवेभ्य आत्मानं निष्क्रीणीते यथैव तत्प्रजापतिर्निरक्रीणीतैवमथ यद्धनिर्निवपति हविषैवैतद्यज्ञं निष्क्रीणाति हविरनुवाकयानुवाक्यामवदानेनावदानं याज्यया याज्यां वसट्कारेण वषट्कारमाहुत्या तस्याहुतिरेवानिष्क्रीता भवति ॥ (*atha yadyajñaṃ tanute | yajñenaivaitaddevebhya ātmānaṃ niṣkrīṇīte yathaiva tatprajāpatirnirakrīṇītaivamatha yaddhanirnivapati haviṣaivaitadyajñaṃ niṣkrīṇāti haviranuvākayānuvākyāmavadānenāvadānaṃ yājyayā yājyāṃ vasaṭkāreṇa vaṣaṭkāramāhutyā tasyāhutirevāniṣkrītā bhavati ‖*) (Śa. Prā. 11-1-8-1) **NOTE**

By this sacrifice the sacrificer repays his debt to the gods. Just as Prajāpati, offering himself as the oblation in the sacrifice performed by himself, gave satisfaction to the gods, so now also the sacrificer must offer himself to the gods in the form of an oblation. (Vows such as fasting are the purport of this.) If the sacrificer spends the period of the consecration with excellent observance of rules and with his senses subdued, it is as if he had given satisfaction to the gods. Thus the necessity of self-sacrifice is declared. Self-sacrifice does not mean suicide; self-renunciation is the aim.

After creation, in the period of continuance, just as the differences of name and form exist, differences of place and time too are necessarily present. The sentence quoted below tells how, just as Viśvakarman pervades all regions (space), the sacrifice also attains unity with heaven and earth and the atmosphere and has identity with them.

उषा वा अश्वस्य मेध्यस्य शिरः । सूर्यश्चक्षुर्वातः प्राणो व्यात्तमग्निर्वैश्वानरः संवत्सर आत्माश्वस्य मेध्यस्य । द्यौः पृष्ठमंतरिक्षमुदरं पृथिविः पाजस्यं दिशः पार्श्वे अवांतरदिशः पर्शव ऋतवोंगानि मासाश्चार्र्धमासाश्च पर्वाण्यहोरात्राणि प्रतिष्ठा नक्षत्राण्यस्थिति नभो मांसानि । (*uṣā vā aśvasya medhyasya śiraḥ | sūryaścakṣurvātaḥ prāṇo vyāttamagnirvaiśvānaraḥ saṃvatsara ātmāśvasya medhyasya | dyauḥ pṛṣṭhamaṃtarikṣamudaraṃ pṛthiviḥ pājasyaṃ diśaḥ pārśve avāṃtaradiśaḥ parśava ṛtavoṃgāni māsāścārrdhamāsāśca parvāṇyahorātrāṇi pratiṣṭhā nakṣatrāṇyasthiti nabho māṃsāni |*)

### Printed p. 232

ऊवध्यं सिकताः सिंधवो गुदा..................यद्विजृंभते तद्विद्योतते यद्विधूनुते तत्स्ननयति यन्मेहति तद्वर्षति वागेवास्य वाक् ॥ (*ūvadhyaṃ sikatāḥ siṃdhavo gudā..................yadvijṛṃbhate tadvidyotate yadvidhūnute tatsnanayati yanmehati tadvarṣati vāgevāsya vāk ‖*) (Bṛ. 81 1-1) **NOTE**

It is declared that Uṣas, the giver of vitality and radiance to the world, is the head of this sacrificial horse; the sun is its eye; the wind is its inhalation and exhalation; the Vaiśvānara fire is its opened mouth; the year is its body; the heaven is its back; the atmosphere is its belly; the earth is its hoof; the directions are its sides; the intermediate directions are its ribs; the seasons and the solstices are its limbs; the months and fortnights are its joints; days and nights are its feet; the stars are its bones; the rivers are its blood-vessels; the trees are its hairs; when it yawns there is lightning; when it neighs there is thunder. Thus the limbs of the sacrificial horse are all space and time. That is, it is declared that the whole universe, divided by name and form, is itself a part of the principle of sacrifice. Therefore everything that is produced grows only in the form of sacrifice. From creation until dissolution, everything that is produced passes through three stages: the stage of the morning, the stage of midday, and the condition of the evening. In every life these three stages exist. All of these have the form of sacrifice, and only if all three are full is life full. How? —

, पुरुषो वाव यज्ञस्तस्य यानि चतुर्विंशतिवर्षाणि तत्प्रातःसवनं चतुर्विंशत्यक्षरा गायत्री प्रातःसवनं तदस्य वसवोऽन्वायत्ताः प्राणा वाव वसव एते हीदं सर्वं वासयंति ॥ (*, puruṣo vāva yajñastasya yāni caturviṃśativarṣāṇi tatprātaḥsavanaṃ caturviṃśatyakṣarā gāyatrī prātaḥsavanaṃ tadasya vasavo 'nvāyattāḥ prāṇā vāva vasava ete hīdaṃ sarvaṃ vāsayaṃti ‖*) (Bhā. U. 3-16-1) **NOTE**

Man himself is the sacrifice. His first twenty-four years are the morning pressing. The Gāyatrī has twenty-four syllables. Therefore the morning pressing must be performed with the help of a mantra in the Gāyatrī metre. The Vasus belong to this morning pressing. The vital breaths of man are these Vasus. Because they make everything here fit to dwell in, the breaths are called Vasus.

तं चेदेतस्मिन्वयसि किंचिदुपतपेत्स ब्रूयात्पाणा वसव इदं मे प्रातःसवनं माध्यंदिनं सवनमनुसंतनुतेति माहं प्राणानां वसूनां मध्ये यज्ञो विलोप्सियेत्युद्धैव तत एत्यगदो ह भवति ॥ (*taṃ cedetasminvayasi kiṃcidupatapetsa brūyātpāṇā vasava idaṃ me prātaḥsavanaṃ mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanamanusaṃtanuteti māhaṃ prāṇānāṃ vasūnāṃ madhye yajño vilopsiyetyuddhaiva tata etyagado ha bhavati ‖*) (Bhā. U. 3-16-2)

If in this period of life man is afflicted by disease, then he should pray: "O breaths, who are the Vasus, let this morning pressing of mine, which is my life, not be cut short here; let it continue on into the midday pressing; let this sacrifice of my life go on." If he prays thus, he is freed from the disease.

### Printed p. 233

अथ यानि चशुश्चत्वांरंशद्वर्षाणि तन्माध्यंदिनं सवनं चतुश्चत्वांरंशदक्षरा त्रिष्टुप् त्रैष्टुभं माध्यंदिनं सवनं तदस्य रुद्रा आन्वायत्ताः प्राणा वाव रुद्रा एते हीदं सर्वं रोदयंति ॥ (*atha yāni caśuścatvāṃraṃśadvarṣāṇi tanmādhyaṃdinaṃ savanaṃ catuścatvāṃraṃśadakṣarā triṣṭup traiṣṭubhaṃ mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanaṃ tadasya rudrā ānvāyattāḥ prāṇā vāva rudrā ete hīdaṃ sarvaṃ rodayaṃti ‖*) (Chā. U. 3-16-3)

The next forty-four years, after the time of the morning pressing, are of the form of the midday pressing. The Triṣṭubh metre has forty-four syllables. Therefore the midday pressing must be performed with the help of a mantra in the Triṣṭubh metre. In this period the vital breaths of man are of the form of the Rudras. Because in time of trouble they make all weep (रोदयंति (*rodayaṃti*)), they are called Rudras.

तं चेदेतस्मिन्वयसि किंचिदुपतपेत्स ब्रूयत् प्राणा रुद्रा इदं मे माध्यंदिनं सवनं तृतीयसवनमनुसंतनुतेति माहं प्राणानां रुद्राणां मध्यॆ यज्ञो विलोप्पीयेत्युद्धैव तत एत्यगदो ह भवति ॥ (*taṃ cedetasminvayasi kiṃcidupatapetsa brūyat prāṇā rudrā idaṃ me mādhyaṃdinaṃ savanaṃ tṛtīyasavanamanusaṃtanuteti māhaṃ prāṇānāṃ rudrāṇāṃ madhyè yajño viloppīyetyuddhaiva tata etyagado ha bhavati ‖*) (Chā. U. 3-16-4)

If man is afflicted by disease in this period of his life, then he should pray: "O breaths, who are of the form of the Rudras, let this sacrifice of mine, which is my midday pressing, continue up to the evening pressing; let it not end here." If he prays thus, man is freed from the disease.

अथ यान्यष्टा चत्वारिंशद्वर्षाणि तत्तृतीयसवनमष्टाचत्वारिंशदक्षरा जगती जागतं तृतीयसवनं तदस्यादित्या अन्वायत्ताः प्राणा वावादित्या एते हीदं सर्ववाददते । (*atha yānyaṣṭā catvāriṃśadvarṣāṇi tattṛtīyasavanamaṣṭācatvāriṃśadakṣarā jagatī jāgataṃ tṛtīyasavanaṃ tadasyādityā anvāyattāḥ prāṇā vāvādityā ete hīdaṃ sarvavādadate |*) (Bhā. U. 3-16-5)

The next forty-eight years are of the form of the evening pressing. The Jagatī metre has forty-eight syllables. Therefore the evening pressing is to be performed with the help of a mantra in the Jagatī form. The Ādityas belong to this pressing. In this period the breaths, in the form of the Ādityas, take hold of everything, and therefore they are called Ādityas.

तं चेदेतस्मिन्वयसि किंचिदुपतपेत्स ब्रूयात् प्राणा आदित्या इदं मे तृतीयसवनमायुरनुसंतनुतेति माहं प्राणानां मादित्यानां मध्यॆ यज्ञो विलोप्सीयेत्युद्धैव तत एत्यगदो हैव भवति ॥ (*taṃ cedetasminvayasi kiṃcidupatapetsa brūyāt prāṇā ādityā idaṃ me tṛtīyasavanamāyuranusaṃtanuteti māhaṃ prāṇānāṃ mādityānāṃ madhyè yajño vilopsīyetyuddhaiva tata etyagado haiva bhavati ‖*) (Chā. U. 3-16-6)

If in this period of man's life he is troubled by disease, then he prays: "O breaths, who are of the nature of the Ādityas, let this oblation of mine, which is my life, go on as long as my full span of life lasts; let it not stop at half the span." Praying thus and so on, he is freed from disease. And further —

### Printed p. 234

स यदशिशिषति यत्पिपासति यान्न रमते ता अस्य दीक्षाः । (*sa yadaśiśiṣati yatpipāsati yānna ramate tā asya dīkṣāḥ |*) (Chā. U. 3-17-1)

As long as man, afflicted by hunger and thirst, does not enjoy pleasure and keeps a discipline, that is the period of consecration in this sacrifice of his life.

अथ यदत्नाति यत्पिबति यद्रमते तदुपसदैरेति ॥ (*atha yadatnāti yatpibati yadramate tadupasadaireti ‖*) (Bhā. U. 3-17-2)

When man partakes of food and drink and enjoys pleasure, he joins the rite called Upasad. (It is a vow of living only on milk and the like. Śrī Śaṅkarācārya has explained: उपसदां च पयोव्रतत्वनिमित्तं सुखमस्ति (*upasadāṃ ca payovratatvanimittaṃ sukhamasti*) **GLOSS**.)

आथ युद्धसति यज्जक्षति यन्मैथुनं चरति स्तुतशस्त्रैरेव तदेति ॥ (*ātha yuddhasati yajjakṣati yanmaithunaṃ carati stutaśastraireva tadeti ‖*) (Chā. U. 3-17-3)

When man laughs with delight and goes on enjoying pleasures, he attains a state equal to the rite of praise with śastras and the like.

अथ यत्तपो दानमार्जवमहिंसा सत्यवचनमिति ता अस्य दक्षिणाः ॥ (*atha yattapo dānamārjavamahiṃsā satyavacanamiti tā asya dakṣiṇāḥ ‖*) (Chā. U. 3-17-4)

Austerity, charity, honesty, non-violence and truthfulness: these are the excellent sacrificial fees that man must offer in this sacrifice of man.

तस्मादाहुः सोष्यत्यसोष्टेति पुनरुत्पादनमेवास्य तन्मरणमेवावभृथः ॥ (*tasmādāhuḥ soṣyatyasoṣṭeti punarutpādanamevāsya tanmaraṇamevāvabhṛthaḥ ‖*) (Chā. U. 3-17-5)

Therefore, it is said of him "he will be born" and "he has been born"; saying that his mother bears him or has borne him is birth. When, after birth, the sacrifice of man is completed, death at the final time is the concluding bath of this sacrifice. (This is the principle taught by Ghora Āṅgirasa to Kṛṣṇa, son of Devakī.)

Thus the individual sacrifice of man described in the Upaniṣads is a reflection of the universal sacrifice of Viśvakarman, who is without beginning and end. The sacrifice of Viśvakarman has no beginning and no end; it goes on continuously. As the fruit of this sacrifice, creation, continuance and dissolution too go on continuously. Therefore, although it is without beginning and end, since every creation is the fruit of its own sacrifice, every sacrifice necessarily has a measure in the form of time or space (Time and Space). For this period in the form of time, the year is the measure (Unit). For the year

### Printed p. 235

the spring, summer and autumn are limbs; in the same way the sacrifice, which is of the nature of the year, has these three limbs. The three, the offering of ghee, the offering of the fuel-stick and the offering of the oblation, reflect these limbs.

Next, Viśvakarman, who is denoted by names such as Prajāpati and Puruṣa and who is of universal form, following the earlier pattern, offers himself as the complete oblation in every sacrifice that is his creation. (The sentences beginning स्वयं यजस्व (*svayaṃ yajasva*) declare this point.) Therefore creation and the rest, which go on continuously in the form of repetition, are themselves the sacrifice.

तस्मात्पश्चाद्वरीयसः प्रजननादिमाः प्रजाः प्रजायंते ॥ (*tasmātpaścādvarīyasaḥ prajananādimāḥ prajāḥ prajāyaṃte ‖*) (Śa. Cā. 3-5-1-11) **NOTE**

The sacrifice is compared to a woman giving birth: from this very extensive female womb, in the form of sacrifice, creatures are born. Everything produced, from the gods and asuras down to the atom of earth, is the fruit of this sacrifice. The performers of this sacrifice are three: (1) Prajāpati, (2) Agni, (3) and the sacrificer in human form. The action of these three must follow one rule. In the time of his individual sacrifice of man, man must follow the sacrificial action of this Viśvakarman (Prajāpati). For that the help of Agni is indispensable. This mutually helpful action is indispensable for this universal course of things to go on without obstruction. This matter is stated in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa as follows.

पुरुषसंमितो यज्ञः । यज्ञः पुरुषसम्मितः । अग्ने तदस्य कल्पय । त्वगंहि वेत्थ यथातथं । यत्पाकत्रा मनसा दीनदक्षान ॥ (*puruṣasaṃmito yajñaḥ | yajñaḥ puruṣasammitaḥ | agne tadasya kalpaya | tvagaṃhi vettha yathātathaṃ | yatpākatrā manasā dīnadakṣāna ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 3-7-11-5)

The sacrifice is of the measure of man. O Agni, you are the one who knows the whole principle of sacrifice. Therefore help man too in the performance of this sacrifice and raise him up. Thus it prays. If man (the sacrificer), knowing this principle of sacrifice, performs his action, then he too attains the measure of this original Puruṣa; that is, one of contracted form, by his sacrificial action, attains expansion and is freed from contraction. This kind of expansion is to be kept as the aim of the sacrifice. Viśvakarman too, having contracted himself, attained by this very sacrifice his expansion in the form of the manifestation of the universe: this is the purport of the present Ṛk.

### Printed p. 236

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वा॒चस्पतिं॑ वि॒श्वक॑र्माणमू॒तये॑ मनो॒जुवं॒ वाजे॑ अ॒द्या  ॑
> हु॑वेम ।  
> स नो॒ विश्वा॑नि॒ हव॑नानि जोषद्वि॒श्वशं॑भू॒रव॑से सा॒धुक॑र्मा ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वाचः । पतिं । विश्वकर्माणं । ऊतये । मनःजुवं । वाजे । अद्य । हुवेम ।  
> सः । नः । विश्वानि । हवनानि । जोषत् । विश्वशंभूः । अवसे । साधुकर्मा ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वाचस्पतिं मंत्रात्मकस्य वचसः स्वामिनं विश्वकर्माणं विश्वकर्तारं मनोजुवं मनोवेगगमनं वाजे यज्ञेऽद्यास्मिन्दिन ऊतये तर्पणाय हुवेम ।
> आह्वयाम । स देवो नोऽस्माकं विश्वानि सर्वाणि हवनानि जोषत् । सेवतां ।
> किमर्थं । अवसेऽस्माकं रक्षणाय । स विशेष्यते । विश्वशंभूर्विश्वस्य सुखस्योत्पादकः
> साधुकर्मा च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

He explains वाचस्पतिं (*vācaspatiṃ*) as the lord of speech in the form of mantras, विश्वकर्माणं (*viśvakarmāṇaṃ*) as the maker of the whole, and मनोजुवं (*manojuvaṃ*) as one whose motion is swift as the mind. वाजे (*vāje*) means in the sacrifice, अद्य (*adya*) on this day, and ऊतये (*ūtaye*) for satisfaction. हुवेम (*huvema*) means "we invoke". That god is to accept (जोषत् (*joṣat*) means "may he serve") all our invocations (हवनानि (*havanāni*)). For what purpose? अवसे (*avase*): for our protection. He is further qualified: विश्वशंभूः (*viśvaśaṃbhūḥ*) is the producer of the happiness of the whole world, and he is also one of good works (साधुकर्मा (*sādhukarmā*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाचस्पतिं (*vācaspatiṃ*)—the lord of words in the form of mantras
- विश्वकर्माणं (*viśvakarmāṇaṃ*)—the maker of the whole world
- मनोजुवं (*manojuvaṃ*)—one who is swift like the mind, that Viśvakarman
- वाजे (*vāje*)—in the sacrifice
- अद्य (*adya*)—now
- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for protection
- हुवेम (*huvema*)—we call
- विश्वशंभूः (*viśvaśaṃbhūḥ*)—one who brings welfare to all
- साधुकर्मा (*sādhukarmā*)—one of excellent action; such
- सः (*saḥ*)—that god Viśvakarman
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- विश्वानि हवनानि (*viśvāni havanāni*)—all invocations
- अवसे (*avase*)—for our protection
- जोषत् (*joṣat*)—may he accept

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

We now call, praying in the sacrifice for protection, Viśvakarman, who is the lord of words in the form of mantras, the maker of the whole world, and swift as the mind. May the god Viśvakarman, who brings welfare to all and is of excellent action, accept (hear) all our invocations for our protection.

### Printed p. 237

##### English translation
Let us this day invoke for our protection the lord of speech, the creator of all, who is swift as thought; may he, the bestower of all happiness, the doer of good works, be propitiated by all our oblations, (so as) to grant us his protection.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The previous Ṛks have praised the self-sacrifice of Viśvakarman as the root cause of the creation, fullness and growth of the world. The same Viśvakarman is praised in the present Ṛk by the epithet वाचस्पतिं विश्वकर्माणं (*vācaspatiṃ viśvakarmāṇaṃ*). It is most fitting in the present context that Viśvakarman, who is of the form of sacrifice and the root cause of creation, continuance and dissolution, is revealed by this epithet. It is quite necessary to examine here the true purport of this epithet. Creation, the sacrifice that is the root of creation, and Viśvakarman, the cause of both: these are the main subject of the present sūkta.

In this context the epithet वाचस्पतिं (*vācaspatiṃ*) makes known three matters. Firstly,

1. 1) The relation between speech and Viśvakarman.
2) The relation of cause and effect between speech and the universal sacrifice of Viśvakarman, who is of the nature of sacrifice.
3) The fixed relation between speech and Viśvakarman's actions such as creation and continuance.

In the present case this matter is examined on the basis of the Vedic sentences that make known these three matters. In explaining the special topics of the Ṛk चत्वारि वाक्परिमिता पदानि........ (*catvāri vākparimitā padāni........*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-45), the divisions of speech such as noun and verb have been explained at length. भूः (*bhūḥ*)

### Printed p. 238

भुवः, सुवः (*bhuvaḥ, suvaḥ*): the nature of speech in the form of the vyāhṛtis has been explained there. (Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Part 12, pp. 615-624.) There was no occasion to explain the matters now in hand in that place. Therefore, as the context requires, the above matters are examined in this section.

Like Viśvakarman, who pervades the three worlds and is of the nature of the three worlds, the goddess of speech too is of the nature of the universe and has the form of the universe. It is because he is her lord that the name Vācaspati is also famous for Viśvakarman. How? —

वाचा वेदा ह्यधीयंते वाचा छंदांसि तत्र ह । अथो वाक्सर्वमेवेदं तेन वाचस्पतिस्तुतः ॥ (*vācā vedā hyadhīyaṃte vācā chaṃdāṃsi tatra ha | atho vāksarvamevedaṃ tena vācaspatistutaḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-45)

It is with the help of speech that the Vedas are recited. The metres too are built upon speech. The whole universe is of the form of speech. Therefore he has the name Vācaspati. This speech itself is also called Sarasvatī.

सरांसि धृतवंत्यस्य संति लोकेषु यत्रिषु । सरस्वंतमिति प्राह वाचं प्राहुः सरस्वतीं ॥ (*sarāṃsi dhṛtavaṃtyasya saṃti lokeṣu yatriṣu | sarasvaṃtamiti prāha vācaṃ prāhuḥ sarasvatīṃ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-51)

(Bṛ. De. 2-50) In the preceding verse there is the derivation of the word Viśvakarman. That same Viśvakarman is also famous as सरस्वान् (*sarasvān*). This name is because he possesses lakes full of ghee in the three worlds. If Viśvakarman is famous as सरस्वान् (*sarasvān*), the goddess of speech is famous as सरस्वती (*sarasvatī*). (It was stated earlier that the epithet सरस्वान् (*sarasvān*) does not refer to Viśvakarman alone; it can designate either Indra, who dwells in the middle world, or the lightning fire.) **NOTE**

The identity of Viśvakarman and Prajāpati has been stated earlier. In the same way, it is known from many sentences that the two epithets Viśvakarman and Bṛhaspati teach one and the same principle. Bṛhaspati is not different, and Brahmaṇaspati is not different. How? —

बृहंतौ पाति यल्लोकावेष द्वौ मध्यमोत्तमौ । बृहता कर्मणा तेन बृहस्पतिरितीळितः ॥ (*bṛhaṃtau pāti yallokāveṣa dvau madhyamottamau | bṛhatā karmaṇā tena bṛhaspatiritīḷitaḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-39)

Because he protects the middle world and the heavenly world, which are exceedingly vast, he has the name Bṛhaspati. Moreover, because he is one of great action, he is also named Bṛhaspati. Thus the derivation of the word Bṛhaspati accords with the great action of Viśvakarman. And likewise —

ब्रह्म वाग्ब्रह्म सत्यं च ब्रह्म सर्वमिदं जगत् । पातारं ब्रह्मणस्तेन शौनहोत्रस्तुवन् जगौ ॥ (*brahma vāgbrahma satyaṃ ca brahma sarvamidaṃ jagat | pātāraṃ brahmaṇastena śaunahotrastuvan jagau ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-40)

Speech is of the form of Brahman. Brahman is of the nature of truth. This whole world is full of Brahman (and therefore full of speech). For this reason Gṛtsamada, son of Śunahotra, praised this primal principle as Brahmaṇaspati.

Just as Viśvakarman, the cause of the world, pervades the three worlds, so the goddess of speech (Brahman, speech), who is of his own nature, pervades the three worlds. All the other deities, such as Uṣas, are but other forms of this goddess of speech. How? —

### Printed p. 239

पार्थिवी मध्यमा दिव्या वागपि त्रिविधा तु या । तस्याः सूक्तानि नामानि यथास्थानं निबोधत ॥ (*pārthivī madhyamā divyā vāgapi trividhā tu yā | tasyāḥ sūktāni nāmāni yathāsthānaṃ nibodhata ‖*) (Bi. De. 2-22) **NOTE**

The goddess of speech pervades the three worlds. She herself, and none other, is the presiding deity of these three, earth, atmosphere and heaven. But her naming and her praise are according to the particular action suited to each world. The order of this is as follows.

कृत्स्नं तु भजते सूक्तं एषा नद्यः स्तुता भुवि । यदा चैनं भजंत्यापो यदा चौषधयो यदा ॥ (*kṛtsnaṃ tu bhajate sūktaṃ eṣā nadyaḥ stutā bhuvi | yadā cainaṃ bhajaṃtyāpo yadā cauṣadhayo yadā ‖*) (Bṛ. Ve. 2-73) **NOTE**

When rivers on the earth, or waters of other forms, or plants are praised by Ṛks, all the Ṛks of that sūkta praise this goddess of speech herself.

अरण्यानी च रात्रि च श्रद्धा चोषाः सरस्वती । पृथिवी चैव नामैषा भूत्वा प्यर्चं भजंति च ॥ (*araṇyānī ca rātri ca śraddhā coṣāḥ sarasvatī | pṛthivī caiva nāmaiṣā bhūtvā pyarcaṃ bhajaṃti ca ‖*)

This Sarasvatī (the goddess of speech), who bears the names अरण्यानी (*araṇyānī*), रात्रिः (*rātriḥ*), श्रद्धा (*śraddhā*), उषाः (*uṣāḥ*), पृथ्वी (*pṛthvī*) and so on, is the object of the praise of the Ṛks.

अग्नाया नामतोऽप्येषा भूत्वाग्नेयेषु केषुचित् । स्तुतः निपातमात्रेण तत्र तत्रेह दृश्यते ॥ (*agnāyā nāmato 'pyeṣā bhūtvāgneyeṣu keṣucit | stutaḥ nipātamātreṇa tatra tatreha dṛśyate ‖*) (Bṛ. Ve. 7-75) **NOTE**

She has also the name अग्नायी (*agnāyī*). In some sūktas related to Agni (1-22-12; 5 46-8) there is praise of this deity.

मध्ये सत्यदितिर्वाक् च भूत्वा चैषा सरस्वती । समग्रं भजते सूक्तं त्रिभिरेव तु नामभिः ॥ (*madhye satyaditirvāk ca bhūtvā caiṣā sarasvatī | samagraṃ bhajate sūktaṃ tribhireva tu nāmabhiḥ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 7-76)

When she dwells in the middle world, the goddess of speech, bearing the names वाक् (*vāk*), अदिति (*aditi*) and सरस्वती (*sarasvatī*), becomes the sharer of the sūkta.

एषैव दुर्गा भूत्वर्चं कृत्वा स्यात्सूक्तभागिनी । तन्नामानि यमींद्राणी सरमा रोमशोर्वशी । भवत्यग्रा सिनीवाली राका चानुमतिः कुहूः । (*eṣaiva durgā bhūtvarcaṃ kṛtvā syātsūktabhāginī | tannāmāni yamīṃdrāṇī saramā romaśorvaśī | bhavatyagrā sinīvālī rākā cānumatiḥ kuhūḥ |*)

### Printed p. 240

गौर्धेनुदर्देवपत्न्योऽघ्ना पथ्या स्वस्तिश्च रोदसी । नौपातिकानि युग्भांजि येषां नामानि कानिचित् ॥ (*gaurdhenudardevapatnyo 'ghnā pathyā svastiśca rodasī | naupātikāni yugbhāṃji yeṣāṃ nāmāni kānicit ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-27, 78)

यमी (*yamī*), इंद्राणी (*iṃdrāṇī*), सरमा (*saramā*), रोमशा (*romaśā*) and उर्वशी (*urvaśī*) are also her names. First, this goddess of speech is indicated by the names सिनीवाली (*sinīvālī*), राका (*rākā*), अनुमति (*anumati*), कुहूः (*kuhūḥ*), गौः (*gauḥ*), धेनु (*dhenu*), देवपत्नी (*devapatnī*), अघ्न्या (*aghnyā*), पथ्यास्वस्ति (*pathyāsvasti*), रोदसी (*rodasī*) and so on.

यदा तु वाग्भवत्येषा सूर्यामुं लोकमाश्रिता । तथा सूक्तमुषा भूत्वा सूर्या च भजतेऽखिलं ॥ (*yadā tu vāgbhavatyeṣā sūryāmuṃ lokamāśritā | tathā sūktamuṣā bhūtvā sūryā ca bhajate 'khilaṃ ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 2-79)

When this goddess of speech is called by the name Sūryā, she dwells in the heavenly world. Dwelling in the heavenly world and shining in the form of Sūryā and in the form of Uṣas, there are entire sūktas that praise her. (The 85th sūkta of the tenth Maṇḍala is concerned with Sūryā. There are many sūktas composed with Uṣas as their subject.) Likewise she is famous by names such as वृषाकपायिा (*vṛṣākapāyiā*), सरण्यू (*saraṇyū*) and गौरी (*gaurī*). Therefore all the goddesses who are praised in the three worlds are other forms of this goddess of speech.

तिस्रस्तु देव्यो याः प्रोक्तास्त्रिस्थानैवेह सा तु वाक् । त्रिविधेनोच्यते नाम्ना ज्योतिषु त्रिषु वर्तिनी ॥ (*tisrastu devyo yāḥ proktāstristhānaiveha sā tu vāk | trividhenocyate nāmnā jyotiṣu triṣu vartinī ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 3-12)

What was said earlier (2-72) is repeated here. But that she has three luminous forms is the special point here. The special forms of this speech are the transformations, in the form of radiance, connected with the three presiding deities of the worlds: Agni, Vāyu or Indra, and Āditya, who exist in the three worlds. How are these three forms? —

अग्निमेवानुगेळा तु मध्यं प्राप्ता सरस्वती । अमुं स्थिताधि लोकं तु भारती भवति ह्यसौ ॥ (*agnimevānugeḷā tu madhyaṃ prāptā sarasvatī | amuṃ sthitādhi lokaṃ tu bhāratī bhavati hyasau ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 3-13)

She follows the earthly Agni in the form of Iḷā. In the form of Sarasvatī she follows Indra of the middle world, or the lightning fire; and, dwelling in the heavenly world, she follows Āditya in the form of Bhāratī.

सैषा तु त्रिविधा वाग्वै दिवि च व्योम्मि चेह च । व्यस्ता चैव समस्ता च भजत्यग्नीनिमानपि ॥ (*saiṣā tu trividhā vāgvai divi ca vyommi ceha ca | vyastā caiva samastā ca bhajatyagnīnimānapi ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 3-15)

### Printed p. 241

The one unity of speech pervades the three worlds in three forms (following the special forms of Viśvakarman). She serves the three forms of Agni, whether separately (in different forms) or in the combined form, and attains identity with them.

It was stated earlier that the three sages Bhṛgu, Aṅgiras and Atri, born of the seed of Varuṇa and Prajāpati, are sons of the goddess of speech (Bṛ. De. 5-98-100). It was also stated, in describing the divine history of those sages, that these three sages possess a divine portion of Agni. That is, it would not be wrong to say that even the divine portions of Agni were born by the power of the goddess of speech. It is because of this universal pervasiveness that, by mantras such as यद्वाग्त्वदंती ; देवीं वाचमजनयंत (*yadvāgtvadaṃtī ; devīṃ vācamajanayaṃta*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-100-10, 11), the all-pervading goddess of speech is praised, as is stated.

वाचं सर्वगतां देवीं स्तौति यद्वागिति द्वृजे ॥ (*vācaṃ sarvagatāṃ devīṃ stauti yadvāgiti dvṛje ‖*) (Bṛ. De. 6-121)

Thus in the Bṛhaddevatā too the greatness of speech is praised. If it is asked whether this goddess of speech is a separate deity or a divine portion of Viśvakarman, the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa gives the following answer.

विश्वकर्मऋषिरिति । वाग्वै विश्वकर्मऋषिनाचा हीदग्ं सर्वं कृतं तस्माद्वाग्विश्वकर्मर्षिः प्रजापतिगृहीतया त्वयेति प्रजापतिसृष्टया त्वयेत्येतद्वाचं गृह्णामि प्रजाभ्य इति वाचमुपरिष्टात्पापादयत..... ॥ (*viśvakarmaṛṣiriti | vāgvai viśvakarmaṛṣinācā hīdagṃ sarvaṃ kṛtaṃ tasmādvāgviśvakarmarṣiḥ prajāpatigṛhītayā tvayeti prajāpatisṛṣṭayā tvayetyetadvācaṃ gṛhṇāmi prajābhya iti vācamupariṣṭātpāpādayata..... ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 8-1-2-9)

Viśvakarman, who does all action, is none other than speech. Nothing in the world is other than what is made by Viśvakarman. Everything that is produced belongs to the dealings in the form of speech. Therefore there is non-difference between Viśvakarman and speech. The sacrificer resolves: "I take up this speech, made by Prajāpati, for the welfare of my creatures." And further —

सा वा एषा वाक्रेधा विहिता । ऋचो यजूग्ंषि सामानि मंडलमेवर्चोर्चिः सामानि पुरुषो यजूग्‌ष्यथैतदमृतं यदेतदर्चिर्दीप्यत इदं तत्पुष्करपर्णं ...... ॥ (*sā vā eṣā vākredhā vihitā | ṛco yajūgṃṣi sāmāni maṃḍalamevarcorciḥ sāmāni puruṣo yajūgṣyathaitadamṛtaṃ yadetadarcirdīpyata idaṃ tatpuṣkaraparṇaṃ ...... ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 10-5-1-5)

### Printed p. 242

Speech is threefold, with the divisions Ṛk, Yajus and Sāman. If complete speech reflects the Puruṣa within the Āditya, who is the soul of the universe, then the Ṛk mantra reflects the orb of the Āditya, the Sāman reflects the radiance, and the Yajus mantras reflect the Puruṣa within the Āditya. Thus speech, as the reflection of Viśvakarman, is all-pervading and all-powerful.

**9. The mutual relation between speech and the principle of sacrifice, the root of creation**

Earlier, in the special topics of the Ṛk 1-164-35 of the Ṛ. Saṃ., the relation between speech and the sacrificial altar, the navel of the world, was explained. The purport of the sentence ब्रह्मायं वाचः परमं व्योम (*brahmāyaṃ vācaḥ paramaṃ vyoma*) was explained. It was explained that Prajāpati is the speech-form of speech, that for him, who has the form of speech, the tongue is the seat and the sky the place of support, and so on. (Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, Part 12.)

The relation of woman and man is the root cause of creation. If the sacrifice is to be the root cause of the creation of the world, that too is possible only through relation with a woman. It is well known that speech is that woman. The relation that arose between the sacrifice, which is of the male form, and speech, which is of the female form, is stated in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa as follows.

देवाश्च वासुराश्नोभये प्राजापत्याः प्रजापतेः पितुर्दायमुपेयुर्मन एव देवा उपायन्वाचमसुरा यज्ञमेव तद्देवा उपायन्वाचमसुराः ॥ (*devāśca vāsurāśnobhaye prājāpatyāḥ prajāpateḥ piturdāyamupeyurmana eva devā upāyanvācamasurā yajñameva taddevā upāyanvācamasurāḥ ‖*) (Śa. Batra. 3-2-1-18) **NOTE**

The gods, born of Prajāpati, and the asuras too, wishing to share the property of their father, tried to obtain the parts of Prajāpati: mind, speech and sacrifice. The gods obtained mind and sacrifice. The asuras obtained speech.

ते देवा यज्ञमब्रुवन् । योषा वा इयं वागुपमंत्रयस्व ह्वयिष्यते वै त्वेति स्वयं ना हैवैक्षत योषा वा इयं वागुपमंत्रय्यॆ............ ॥ (*te devā yajñamabruvan | yoṣā vā iyaṃ vāgupamaṃtrayasva hvayiṣyate vai tveti svayaṃ nā haivaikṣata yoṣā vā iyaṃ vāgupamaṃtrayyè............ ‖*) (Pa. Cā. 3-2-1-19) **NOTE**

When the gods said, "O sacrifice, you are of the male form. If you gain the relation of speech, who is of the female form, you will shine. If you show her your wish and call her, she will call you to herself," the sacrifice too agreed to this. When it called speech accordingly, at first speech refused this invitation. When the sacrifice told this to the gods,

### Printed p. 243

ते होचुः । उपैव भगवो मंत्रयस्व । ह्वयिष्यते वै त्वेति त्वामुपामंत्रयत ॥ (*te hocuḥ | upaiva bhagavo maṃtrayasva | hvayiṣyate vai tveti tvāmupāmaṃtrayata ‖*) (Śa. Prā. 3-2-1-20) **NOTE**

"Approach speech and call her. Such is the nature of woman: at first she refuses," they said. The sacrifice then approached speech, who is of the female form, and called her. Speech only shook her head and so indicated her consent. When it called again, speech consented fully. Then separation arose between speech and the asuras. The sacrifice joined with speech.

सोऽयं यज्ञो वाचमभिदध्यौ । मिथुन्येनया स्यामिति ताग्ं संबभूव (*so 'yaṃ yajño vācamabhidadhyau | mithunyenayā syāmiti tāgṃ saṃbabhūva*) (Śa. Brā. 3-2-1-25)

As soon as the consent of speech was obtained, the desire arose in the sacrifice to join with speech and enjoy. Accordingly both of them united. Then —

इंद्रो ह वा ईक्षांचक्रे । महद्वा इतोऽभ्वं जनिष्यते यज्ञस्य च मिथुनाद्वाचश्च यन्मा तन्नाभिभवेदिति स इंद्र एव गर्भो भूत्वैतन्मिथुनं प्रविवेश ॥ (*iṃdro ha vā īkṣāṃcakre | mahadvā ito 'bhvaṃ janiṣyate yajñasya ca mithunādvācaśca yanmā tannābhibhavediti sa iṃdra eva garbho bhūtvaitanmithunaṃ praviveśa ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 3-2-1-26)

As soon as Indra learned of the union of speech and sacrifice, he thought: "As a result of their being together, a great asura power will surely be born. It will surely have the power to defeat me. Without giving it any opportunity, I myself will enter the womb of it." So he entered that couple in the form of an embryo. It is stated that this is the root of the creation of the world. How? —

स प्रजापतिरिंद्रं पुत्रमब्रवीत् । अनेन त्वा कामप्रेण यज्ञेन यजयानि येन मामिदं परमेष्ठ्ययायजदिति तथेति तमयाजयित्स इष्टाकामयताहमेवेदग्ं सर्वं स्यामिति स वागभवद्वाग्वा इदग्ं सर्वं तस्मादाहुरिंद्रो वागिति ॥ ( (*sa prajāpatiriṃdraṃ putramabravīt | anena tvā kāmapreṇa yajñena yajayāni yena māmidaṃ parameṣṭhyayāyajaditi tatheti tamayājayitsa iṣṭākāmayatāhamevedagṃ sarvaṃ syāmiti sa vāgabhavadvāgvā idagṃ sarvaṃ tasmādāhuriṃdro vāgiti ‖ (*) (Śa. Brā. 11-1-6-18)

Prajāpati spoke thus to his son Indra. When he said, "O Indra, I wish to perform for your welfare this sacrifice, granting what is desired, which Parameṣṭhin performed with me as its object," Indra assented. As soon as that sacrifice was performed, Indra desired to become himself the soul of all and all-pervading. Immediately he attained the form of speech, all-pervading and the soul of all. It is shown that Indra is none other than speech, that is, identity.

### Printed p. 244

**4. Speech is the support of the creation of the world. Viśvakarman, the lord of speech, is the root cause.** **NOTE**

वाचो वै प्रजा विश्वकर्मा जजान (*vāco vai prajā viśvakarmā jajāna*) (Śa. Brā. 2-5-7-21)

Viśvakarman created all creatures with the help of speech alone: thus it is shown that speech is the support. The order of this creation is stated as follows.

स मनसा वाचं मिथुनग्० समभवत् । सोऽष्टौ द्रप्सान् गर्भ्यभवत्तेऽष्टौ वसवोऽसृज्यंत तानस्यामुपादधात् ॥ (*sa manasā vācaṃ mithunag0 samabhavat | so 'ṣṭau drapsān garbhyabhavatte 'ṣṭau vasavo 'sṛjyaṃta tānasyāmupādadhāt ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6-1-2-6)

Viśvakarman, who has the form of Prajāpati, joined with speech in the form of meditation. Then he conceived, together with eight drops of his seed. As a result the eight Vasus were produced. He established them on the earth.

स मनसैव वाचं मिथुनग्ं समभवत्स एकादश द्रप्सान्‌गर्भ्यभवत्त एकादश रुद्रा असृज्यंत तानंतरिक्ष उपादधात् ॥ (*sa manasaiva vācaṃ mithunagṃ samabhavatsa ekādaśa drapsāngarbhyabhavatta ekādaśa rudrā asṛjyaṃta tānaṃtarikṣa upādadhāt ‖*) (Pa. Brā. 6-1-2-7) **NOTE**

Again, in the form of meditation, he joined with speech and was united with her. Then he conceived, together with eleven drops of his seed. From that eleven Rudras were produced. He established these eleven Rudras in the atmosphere.

स मनसैव वाचं मिथुनग्ं समभवत्स द्वादश द्रप्सान्‌गर्भ,भवत्ते द्वादशादित्या असृज्यंत तान् दिव्युपादधात् ॥ (*sa manasaiva vācaṃ mithunagṃ samabhavatsa dvādaśa drapsāngarbha,bhavatte dvādaśādityā asṛjyaṃta tān divyupādadhāt ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 6.1.2-8)

Again, in the form of meditation, he entered into relation with speech. Then he conceived, together with twelve drops of his seed. From that twelve Ādityas were produced. He established these twelve Ādityas in the heavenly world.

स मनसैव वाचं मिथुनग्ं समभवत्स गर्भ्यभवत्स विश्वान् देवानसृजत तान् दिक्षोपादधात् ॥ (*sa manasaiva vācaṃ mithunagṃ samabhavatsa garbhyabhavatsa viśvān devānasṛjata tān dikṣopādadhāt ‖*) (Ca. Jā. 6-1-2-9) **NOTE**

Again, in the form of meditation, he came into union with speech. Then too he conceived. As a result the Viśvedevas were produced. He established them as the guardians of the directions.
